## II.21 Coprime Virasoro Algebra (Non-Normative Research/Historical Branch)

**Source:** §12.1, §12.2, Def 12.1, Thm 12.2, Cor 12.3, Rmk 12.3a, Thm 12.4, Thm 12.5, Rmk 12.6, Thm 12.7 (v2.7.14).  
**v3.0 location:** Part II / II.21.  
**Status:** non-normative research/history (v3.2.0 quarantine).

> **NON-NORMATIVE RESEARCH/HISTORICAL BRANCH.** The Section 12 material is preserved for provenance, but it is removed from active theorem status. Individual items below are reclassified as arithmetic fact, proposed construction, open dependency, superseded statement, or false as stated. Do not use this branch as release-authority for FVLT v3.2.0.

The Witt algebra **witt** has generators $\{L_m : m \in \mathbb{Z}\}$ with brackets $[L_m, L_n] = (m - n) L_{m+n}$. The coprime restriction installs an arithmetic obstruction.

<!-- id: DEF-12.1-COPRIME-WITT-SUBALGEBRA | register: proposed | status: proposed-construction | provenance: original | depends: none -->
**Definition 12.1 (Coprime Witt Span, proposed construction; old 'subalgebra' name superseded).** For modulus $n$:
$$
\mathrm{witt}_n^{\mathrm{cop}} \;=\; \mathrm{span}\{L_k : \gcd(k, n) = 1\}.
$$
This is **not** a subalgebra in general: the bracket $[L_j, L_k]$ lands at index $j + k$, which may not be coprime to $n$.

<!-- id: THM-12.2-SUMSET-OBSTRUCTION-AT-N-720 | register: arithmetic-fact | status: active-arithmetic-fact | provenance: original | depends: none -->
**Arithmetic Fact 12.2 (Sumset Obstruction at $n = 720$, retained).** Let $U = K(720)$. Then $(U + U) \cap U = \emptyset$. Every sum of two elements of $U$ is even, hence divisible by $2$, hence not coprime to $720$.

*Proof.* All elements of $K(720)$ are odd (since $2 \mid 720$ implies every coprime is odd). The sum of two odd integers is even. An even integer shares factor $2$ with $720$. Hence no sum of coprimes is coprime. $\square$

<!-- id: COR-12.3-COPRIME-VIRASORO-QUOTIENT-ABELIAN | register: superseded | status: open-dependency | provenance: original | depends: 12.2 -->
**Superseded Statement 12.3 (quotient abelianness not active).** The legacy claim that $\mathrm{witt}_{720}^{\mathrm{cop}} / I_{720}$ is abelian is not active in v3.2.0: $I_{720}$ is not defined here as an ideal with the needed quotient properties.

<!-- id: RMK-12.3a-EVEN-N-SUMSET-OBSTRUCTION | register: arithmetic-fact | status: active-arithmetic-fact | provenance: original | depends: 12.2 -->
**Remark 12.3a (Even-$N$ sumset obstruction, arithmetic fact retained).** This is not specific to $720$. The sumset obstruction $(U + U) \cap U = \emptyset$ holds for every even $N$, since coprime residues mod any even integer are all odd. The stronger quotient conclusion is not active without a defined quotient/ideal. The $S_6$ material remains in its own group/factorial register (Part V / V.16).

<!-- id: THM-12.4-HEISENBERG-STRUCTURE | register: open-dependency | status: open | provenance: original | depends: 12.3 -->
**Open Dependency 12.4 (Heisenberg Structure, not active).** If a future project defines and verifies the required quotient/central extension, it may ask whether the quotient algebra is isomorphic to a generalized Heisenberg algebra of dimension $\varphi(720) + 1 = 193$, with
$$
[L_k, L_{-k}] \;=\; \frac{c}{12}(k^3 - k) C, \qquad [L_j, L_k] \;=\; 0 \text{ for } j + k \neq 0.
$$

<!-- id: THM-12.5-NO-SL-EMBEDDING | register: open-dependency | status: open | provenance: original | depends: 12.2, 12.3 -->
**Open Dependency 12.5 (No $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ Embedding, not active).** The no-embedding claim depends on the unproved quotient algebra and is not active theorem authority in v3.2.0.

<!-- id: RMK-12.6-STRUCTURAL-FEATURE-OF-OBSTRUCTION | register: arithmetic-fact | status: interpretive | provenance: original | depends: 12.3a -->
**Remark 12.6 (Structural feature of the obstruction, scoped).** The sumset obstruction arises because $K(n)$ for even $n$ consists entirely of odd residues. The further gauge-algebra conclusion is research-only pending the quotient definitions above.

<!-- id: THM-12.7-DEGENERATION-TRICHOTOMY | register: research-proposal | status: research | provenance: original | depends: 12.2, 12.4, 12.5, 13.15, 13.3, 13.5, 13.6, 14.2, 14.3, 14.4c, 3.14, 3.16, 3.17, 8.40 -->
**Research Proposal 12.7 (Degeneration Trichotomy, not active).** The even-modulus sumset obstruction and the legacy Heisenberg proposal (Thm 12.4) respond differently to the three limit operations of the framework (Part VII / VII.5). Only the parity arithmetic is active in v3.2.0; the algebra column below is historical/research shorthand.

| Limit | Sumset obstruction | Algebra | Diagnostic content |
|---|---|---|---|
| $\rho \to 0$ (Vernier) | Preserved | legacy Heisenberg proposal, not active | Clinamen sector empties |
| $k! \to \infty$ (factorial) | Preserved at every level | legacy pro-Heisenberg proposal, not active | Modes grow; parity fixed |
| $p \to \infty$ (prime) | Vanishes | legacy Witt-recovery proposal, not active | Full bracket structure not established here |
| $\xi \in \mathbb{R}$ (continuous) | Undefined | No algebra | No discrete structure |

The factorial and prime limits diverge because factorials are even and (odd) primes are odd: the parity of $N$ is the bifurcation point. The Witt algebra can never be reached along the factorial path.

*Status: research/provisional in v3.2.0. The old unconditional status is superseded because several referenced items are now open dependencies: Vernier from Theorem 3.14 + Theorem 13.3 (Part II / II.5 + II.22); factorial from parity of $k!$ + Theorem 12.2; prime from Cauchy–Davenport + Theorem 14.2 (Part VII / VII.5); continuous from Remark 8.40 (Part II / II.4 limit-discussion).*

## II.22 Coprime Fock-Space Representation (Non-Normative Research/Historical Branch)

**Source:** §13 entirety: §13.1–§13.8 + Summary (v2.7.14).  
**v3.0 location:** Part II / II.22.  
**Status:** non-normative research/history (v3.2.0 quarantine).

> **NON-NORMATIVE RESEARCH/HISTORICAL BRANCH.** The Section 13 material is preserved for provenance but removed from active theorem status. It must not be used as a Fock theorem, Stone-von Neumann theorem, number-operator theorem, or Hasse-Hilbert identification in this baseline.

Per Rule 10 (do not collapse §13 into Rmk 9.7j), the full §13 Fock-branch content is carried as provenance here, independent of the crystal-graph readout of Part II / II.17.

<!-- id: RMK-II.22a-QUANTUM-FORMAL-REGISTER-DISCIPLINE | register: remark | status: interpretive | provenance: original | depends: II.23.2, II.3a, II.3b, 8.19, 7.1, 5.3, II.22 -->
**Remark II.22a (Quantum-Formal Register Discipline, v3.1.21, new, interpretive).**

FVLT is **quantum-formal, not quantum-physical**. The active baseline uses complex amplitudes, conjugation, inner products, Parseval grading for exact-conductor subspaces, and tensor-product factorization where defined. Fock-space occupation and canonical commutation structure are preserved only as research/historical proposals in this branch. This is not a claim that divisor lattices are physical quantum systems, nor that FVLT inherits physical resources — wavefunction collapse, particle ontology, or quantum-computational superposition. The claim is formal: the same Hilbert/Fock/tensor machinery appears as a measurement language for arithmetic channels.

In this register several correspondences are identities, not metaphors:

| Quantum-formal structure | FVLT instantiation | Kernel locus | Status grade |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Superposition over basis modes | A signal decomposes across exact-conductor subspaces indexed by divisors $q \mid N$; Parseval decomposes total energy by $E_q^{\mathrm{full}}$ | Harmonic Hilbert register; Thm 4.6 | active for $H_{\mathrm{harm}}(N)$ |
| Occupation / measurement energy | proposed top-conductor occupation reading | Legacy Thm 13.12; Cor. 13.13 | research-only / not active |
| Bra/ket conjugation | The Vernier gearbox walls form an exact conjugate pair $z_2(s) = \overline{z_1(s)}$ at every integer signal index for integer $t_1$; hence $z_1 z_2 = \lvert z_1\rvert^2$ (Hermitian norm) is exact on that locus. The canonical $t_1 = t_2 = 1$ curve is the $(N{-}1, N{+}1)$ gearbox; extends to real $n_3$ (Rmk 7.1) | Lem. II.23.2 | proved, unconditional (exact on the integer-$s$, integer-$t_1$ locus) |
| Tensor product / composite system | Prime-power axes factor the signature register; $C_N \cong \bigotimes_{p^a \,\Vert\, N} C_{p^a}$, equivalently $C_N(q,\sigma) = \prod_i C_{p_i^{a_i}}(e_i, s_i)$ | Rmk II.3a; Rmk II.3b; Thm 8.19 | theorem-backed / unconditional (boxed in II.3b) |
| Entanglement | Would require non-factorizing amplitudes or states across prime axes | not established | open / not claimed |

The tensor-product row is **not** an entanglement claim. The CRT / prime-axis factorization demonstrates *separability*: it supplies the arena in which an entanglement analogue could be posed, but does not exhibit entanglement. A genuine FVLT entanglement analogue would require a signal, state, or channel-amplitude distribution that fails to factor across the prime-power axes.

Notation: generic quantum amplitudes are written $\lvert\psi\rangle = \sum_j c_j \lvert j\rangle$, **not** $\sum_j \alpha_j \lvert j\rangle$ — $\alpha$ is the FVLT scale exponent (v3.1.20 lock). (The kernel's own $\alpha_j(x)$ for coprime amplitudes in Def 13.8 / Thm 13.12 is a distinct residual collision, deferred to a later local notation pass — see changelog.)

**Disciplined statement.** FVLT instantiates quantum-formal mathematics from divisibility, but does not claim quantum physics. Superposition, conjugation, and declared tensor-product structures are formal tools in the arithmetic measurement register; occupation/Fock claims are research-only, and physical quantum interpretation remains out of scope.

### II.22.1 Normalized generators

<!-- id: DEF-13.1-COPRIME-MODE-PAIRS | register: arithmetic-fact | status: active-arithmetic-fact | provenance: original | depends: none -->
**Definition 13.1 (Coprime Mode Pairs, arithmetic count retained).** The coprime mode set is
$$
\mathcal{M}(N) \;=\; \{k \in K(N) : 1 \leq k \leq N/2\}.
$$
For $N = 720$: $\varphi(720)=192$ and paired modes $=96$. For $N = 1001$: $\varphi(1001)=720$ and paired modes $=360$. For $N = 720720$: $\varphi(720720)=138240$ and paired modes $=69120$. Each $k_j \in \mathcal{M}(N)$ pairs with $N - k_j$ when the pairing convention is defined.

<!-- id: DEF-13.2-NORMALIZED-OPERATORS | register: proposed-construction | status: false-as-stated | provenance: original | depends: 13.1 -->
**Definition 13.2 (Normalized Creation and Annihilation Operators, proposed; singular as stated).** For each $k_j \in \mathcal{M}(N)$ the legacy formula was:
$$
a_j \;=\; \frac{L_{N-k_j}}{\sqrt{(c/12)(k_j^3 - k_j)}}, \qquad a_j^\dagger \;=\; \frac{L_{k_j}}{\sqrt{(c/12)(k_j^3 - k_j)}}.
$$

**v3.2.0 singularity note:** the denominator $k_j^3-k_j$ vanishes at $k_j=1$, and $1 \in \mathcal{M}(720)$. Therefore this normalization is singular as stated and cannot support an active CCR theorem.

<!-- id: THM-13.3-CCR | register: open-dependency | status: open | provenance: original | depends: 12.2, 12.4 -->
**Open Dependency 13.3 (Canonical Commutation Relations, not active).** The proposed normalized operators would have to satisfy
$$
[a_j, a_k^\dagger] \;=\; \delta_{jk} C, \qquad [a_j, a_k] \;=\; [a_j^\dagger, a_k^\dagger] \;=\; 0
$$
for all $j, k \in \{1, \ldots, 96\}$.

*Legacy proof sketch, not active.* Direct substitution into the proposed Heisenberg bracket (Open Dependency 12.4) would be required. The cross-bracket argument also depends on quotient structure beyond the active parity arithmetic. $\square$

<!-- id: RMK-13.4-SUMSET-ROLE | register: research-history | status: historical-provisional | provenance: original | depends: 12.2, 13.3 -->
**Remark 13.4 (Role of the Sumset Obstruction, historical/provisional).** The vanishing of all off-diagonal brackets is a direct consequence of $(U + U) \cap U = \emptyset$. Without this arithmetic constraint, cross-mode couplings would arise, and the algebra would not be Heisenberg. The evenness of $K(720)$-sums — forced by $2 \mid 720$ — is the mechanism that decouples the $96$ modes. The CCR inheritance claim is not active in v3.2.0 pending repair of the quotient and normalization.

### II.22.2 The Fock space

<!-- id: DEF-13.5-COPRIME-FOCK-SPACE | register: proposed-construction | status: research-proposal | provenance: original | depends: 13.3 -->
**Definition 13.5 (Coprime Fock Space, proposed construction).** Let $w_1, \ldots, w_{96}$ be formal variables indexed by $\mathcal{M}(720)$. The Fock space is the symmetric algebra
$$
\mathcal{F}(N) \;=\; \mathbb{C}[w_1, \ldots, w_{96}]
$$
with $a_j^\dagger \mapsto w_j \cdot$ and $a_j \mapsto \partial/\partial w_j$. The vacuum state is $|0\rangle = 1 \in \mathcal{F}(N)$.

<!-- id: THM-13.6-STONE-VON-NEUMANN | register: open-dependency | status: open | provenance: classical | depends: 13.3, 13.5, 13.7 -->
**Open Dependency 13.6 (Stone–von Neumann at Factorial Level, not active).** No Stone–von Neumann conclusion is invoked in v3.2.0 because the Heisenberg algebra and representation hypotheses have not been established for this branch.

<!-- id: RMK-13.7-BARGMANN-FOCK | register: research-history | status: historical-provisional | provenance: classical | depends: 13.5, 13.6 -->
**Remark 13.7 (Bargmann–Fock realization, proposed/historical).** This is the Bargmann–Fock realization [Foc32, Bar61]. The polynomial $w_1^{n_1} \cdots w_{96}^{n_{96}}$ represents the state with $n_j$ excitations in mode $k_j$.

### II.22.3 Signal-to-state map

<!-- id: DEF-13.8-COPRIME-AMPLITUDE | register: proposed-construction | status: research-proposal | provenance: original | depends: 13.1 -->
**Definition 13.8 (Coprime Amplitude, preserved).** For signal $x[t]$ of length $L$ and coprime mode $k_j$:
$$
\alpha_j(x) \;=\; \frac{1}{L} \widehat{X}(k_j/N), \qquad \widehat{X}(f) \;=\; \sum_{t=0}^{L-1} x[t] e^{-2\pi i f t}.
$$

<!-- id: DEF-13.9-COHERENT-STATE | register: proposed-construction | status: research-proposal | provenance: original | depends: 13.5, 13.8 -->
**Definition 13.9 (Coherent State Encoding, proposed; restricted).** The forward map $\Phi_{+1}: \ell^2(\mathbb{Z}_L) \to \mathcal{F}(N)$:
$$
|x\rangle \;=\; \exp\!\left(\sum_{j=1}^{96} \alpha_j(x) a_j^\dagger\right) |0\rangle.
$$
The map is at most a proposed encoding of the coprime-mode subspace; signals differing in non-coprime harmonics map to the same state, so it is not a general signal encoding.

<!-- id: RMK-13.10-ALPHA-PARAMETER-RELATION | register: research-history | status: historical-provisional | provenance: original | depends: 5.1, 5.2, 13.9 -->
**Remark 13.10 (Relation to $\alpha$-Parameter, historical/provisional).** The amplitude exponent $\alpha$ (Part II / II.7 Definition 5.1) was proposed to govern the *scale* of the encoding: $\alpha = +1$ forward (amplitudes $N \alpha_j$), $\alpha = 0$ kernel (unit amplitude), $\alpha = -1$ inverse ($\alpha_j / N$). The transform duality $Z(s; +1) \cdot Z(s; -1) = |Z(s; 0)|^2$ would become the coherent-state identity $\langle x; -1 | x; +1 \rangle = \|x; 0\|^2$ only after the coherent-state construction is supplied.

### II.22.4 Subspace energy as number operator

<!-- id: DEF-13.11-MODE-NUMBER-OPERATOR | register: proposed-construction | status: research-proposal | provenance: original | depends: 13.2, 13.5 -->
**Definition 13.11 (Mode Number Operator, proposed construction).** In the legacy branch, $\hat{n}_j = a_j^\dagger a_j$ and $\hat{N}_{\mathrm{cop}} = \sum_{j=1}^{96} \hat{n}_j$. This is not active until the proposed operators and Fock construction are supplied.

<!-- id: THM-13.12-E_Q-AS-NUMBER-OPERATOR | register: open-dependency | status: open | provenance: original | depends: DEF-ENERGY-FULL, 13.5, 13.11 -->
**Open Dependency 13.12 ($E_q$ as Number Operator, not active).** The legacy claim attempted to identify top-conductor energy with total coprime occupation:
$$
E_N^{\mathrm{full}}(x) \;\stackrel{?}{=}\; \langle x | \hat{N}_{\mathrm{cop}} | x \rangle \;=\; \sum_{j=1}^{96} |\alpha_j(x)|^2.
$$
This is research-only: $E_N^{\mathrm{full}}$ and $E_N^{\mathrm{line}}$ must be distinguished, and the proposed Fock representation is not established.

Translating it into a Fock-space grading requires a future Fock construction and is not established here.

### II.22.5 The pipeline as Fock-space operations

<!-- id: DEF-13.14-PINCH-PROJECTION | register: proposed-construction | status: research-proposal | provenance: original | depends: 13.5 -->
**Definition 13.14 (Pinch Projection, proposed construction).** A pinch point at $s_* = N(k + 1/2)$ corresponds to the envelope zero where all energy resides in the clinamen channel. The legacy branch proposed a pinch projector $\Pi_{\mathrm{pinch}}$ acting on a Fock space by projecting onto the clinamen sector; that Fock-space action is not active in v3.2.0.

<!-- id: THM-13.15-UNITARITY-LIFTING | register: open-dependency | status: open | provenance: original | depends: 4.6, 13.5, 13.14 -->
**Open Dependency 13.15 (Unitarity Lifting, not active).** The legacy claim asserted that the Vernier unitarity identity (Part II / II.6 Thm 4.6) lifts to Fock space: the per-mode energy partition decomposes as
$$
|\alpha_j|^2 \;=\; |\alpha_j^{(Z)}|^2 + |\alpha_j^{(C)}|^2
$$
with $|\alpha_j^{(C)}|^2 = |\alpha_j|^2 \sin^2(\pi k_j / N)$ in the clinamen sector. The total identity $|Z|^2 + |C|^2 = 4$ holds.

<!-- id: RMK-13.16-WHY-FOCK | register: research-history | status: historical-provisional | provenance: original | depends: none -->
**Remark 13.16 (Why the Fock Proposal Was Considered, historical/interpretive).** The legacy branch proposed that the pipeline could inherit representation-theoretic guarantees. In v3.2.0 those guarantees are not active: Stone–von Neumann, number-operator structure, and monoidal lift all depend on unrepaired branch claims.

### II.22.6 Monoidal structure in Fock space

<!-- id: THM-13.17-COHERENT-STATE-TENSOR-PRODUCT | register: open-dependency | status: open | provenance: original | depends: 11.3, 13.5, 13.9 -->
**Open Dependency 13.17 (Coherent State Tensor Product, not active).** The legacy claim asserted that the monoidal functor property (Part II / II.20 Thm 11.3) lifts to Fock space: for concatenated signals $x \otimes x'$,
$$
|x \otimes x'\rangle \;=\; |x\rangle \otimes |x'\rangle.
$$
This inherits the downgraded monoidal/window claim and is not active in v3.2.0.

<!-- id: RMK-13.18-FOURIER-FOCK-CONTRAST | register: remark | status: interpretive | provenance: original | depends: none -->
**Remark 13.18 (Contrast with Fourier–Fock, historical/provisional).** In standard QFT, the Fock space is built from plane-wave modes $e^{ikx}$ with continuous rotational symmetry. The legacy FVLT branch proposed a Fock space built from coprime harmonics $e^{2\pi i k t/N}$ with discrete multiplicative symmetry. The contrast remains a historical research prompt; no FVLT Fock construction is active in v3.2.0.

### II.22.7 Primorial classification in Fock space

<!-- id: THM-13.19-OCCUPANCY-REGIME | register: research-proposal | status: research | provenance: original | depends: 8.8, 13.5 -->
**Research Proposal 13.19 (Occupancy Regime Classification, not active).** For operating shell $N$ with primorial sampling $M = \prod B$, the coprime sector occupancy varies inversely with $|\Sigma(M, N)|$. Geometric richness correlates with coprime compression efficiency; arithmetic richness correlates with divisor-layer diagnostics.

<!-- id: RMK-13.20-FOCK-DUALITY | register: research-history | status: historical-provisional | provenance: original | depends: 8.8 -->
**Remark 13.20 (Geometric–Arithmetic Inversion as Fock Duality, research/historical).** The legacy branch proposed reading Thm 8.8(b) — the inverse correlation between geometric coverage and arithmetic resolution — as a statement about which sector of a Fock space carries energy versus how finely that sector is internally structured. This remains a historical research prompt, not an active energy-conservation or Fock-duality theorem.

<!-- id: RMK-13.21-PRACTICAL-DIAGNOSTIC | register: research-history | status: historical-provisional | provenance: original | depends: none -->
**Remark 13.21 (Practical Diagnostic, research/historical).** The legacy diagnostic proposal was to compute $\Sigma(M, N)$ from $\gcd(M, N)$, read off the geometric class, and determine before any Fock computation whether a coprime compressor or the full divisor decomposition is the appropriate tool. The non-Fock geometric-class readout remains separately governed by its active kernel loci.

## II.23 Measurement Instrument

**Source:** v3.1.7 (measurement-layer precipitation, 2026-05-20 session). Predecessor anchors: Rmk 1.5b (Coordinate vs Closure-of-Description Substrate, Part II / II.2 + Part II / II.19); Rmk 1.5c (Same Divisor, Two Readouts, Part II / II.2); Rmk 1.6b (Three Observation Contexts, Part II / II.19); §5.4 (Geometry vs Measurement, Part II / II.9); II.5 (Vernier interference system); III.11 (Goldbach resonance atlas); ALG-1, ALG-2, ALG-3 (Part VII / VII.2).
**v3.0 location:** Part II / II.23 (new in v3.1.7).
**Status:** definitional / unconditional / interpretive / empirical (per-block).

The measurement instrument names the operational layer between the substrate (Parts I–V) and applications (Part VI). The substrate fixes what is measurable; the instrument names how measurements are taken and scored. Rmk 1.5b / Rmk 1.6b distinguish coordinate, closure, and description substrates with their respective observation contexts; §5.4 distinguishes the geometry register from the measurement register. II.23 sits in the measurement register, in the third observation context (Rmk 1.6b): instruments applied to data over a declared operating shell, with $\mathrm{Div}(720)$ only as the worked 720-shell instance.

### II.23.1 Measurement primitives

<!-- id: DEF-II.23.1-MEASUREMENT-PRIMITIVES | register: definitional | status: definitional | provenance: original | depends: II.4, II.8, II.13 -->
**Definition II.23.1 (Measurement primitives, v3.1.7, new).** Let $x$ be a signal on $\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}$ at operating shell $N$, with supercharacter readouts $\{c_q(x)\}_{q \mid N}$ (Part II / II.2, II.8) and trilattice partition $\{N-1, N, N+1\}$ (Part II / II.4). Define the following operational readouts:

**(i) Spine adherence γ.** Geometric fraction of trajectory length that flows along the spine of the trilattice (the prime walls $N \pm 1$ when prime) versus across the center $N$:
$$
\gamma(x) \;=\; \frac{\text{path length within } \{c_{N-1}, c_{N+1}\} \text{ subspace}}{\text{total path length}}.
$$
Range $\gamma \in [0,1]$. High γ = data locks to a μ-class wall; low γ = data flows across center.

**(ii) Trilattice entropy $H_T$.** Shannon entropy of the trilattice Möbius distribution. With $\mu$-class counts $n_- = |\{i : \mu = -1\}|$, $n_0 = |\{i : \mu = 0\}|$, $n_+ = |\{i : \mu = +1\}|$, total $T = n_- + n_0 + n_+$:
$$
H_T(x) \;=\; -\sum_{c \in \{-, 0, +\}} \frac{n_c}{T} \log_2 \frac{n_c}{T}.
$$
Range $H_T \in [0, \log_2 3]$. $H_T = 0$ = perfect locking to one μ-class; $H_T = \log_2 3$ = uniform across three classes.

**(iii) Harmonic Contrast.** For trilattice $(N-1, N, N+1)$, the totient-ratio
$$
\mathrm{HC}(N) \;=\; \frac{\varphi(N-1) + \varphi(N+1)}{2\,\varphi(N)}.
$$
HC reads how much room the composite center $N$ makes for the walls $N \pm 1$. Values: $\mathrm{HC}(30) = 29/8 = 3.625$ (wide channel, primorial center); $\mathrm{HC}(38) = 5/3 \approx 1.667$ (narrow channel). HC is a structural property of $N$ independent of the data $x$; it sets the measurement-quality envelope at that shell.

**(iv) Time arrow $\Delta|c|$.** Discrete derivative of channel magnitude across step:
$$
\Delta|c|(s) \;=\; |c_{N}(x)|_{\text{at } s+1} - |c_{N}(x)|_{\text{at } s}.
$$
$\Delta|c| > 0$ = channel accumulating energy; $\Delta|c| < 0$ = releasing. Sign-pattern over $s$ is the local time arrow at the central channel.

**(v) μ-class assignment.** For each signal index $s$, assign the dominant Ramanujan sum among $\{c_{N-1}(x), c_N(x), c_{N+1}(x)\}$:
$$
\mu\text{-class}(s) \;=\; \arg\max_{q \in \{N-1, N, N+1\}} |c_q(x_{\text{at } s})|.
$$
The μ-class assignment partitions the signal trajectory into three subtrajectories, one per wall / center. The triple $(n_-, n_0, n_+)$ above is this partition's cardinality vector.

These five primitives are the operational scoring layer for any signal $x$ passed through the FVLT instrument at shell $N$. They are definitional, not theorem-bearing.

### II.23.2 Vernier conjugate-pair lemma

<!-- id: LEM-II.23.2-VERNIER-CONJUGATE-PAIR | register: proved | status: unconditional | provenance: original | depends: II.5 -->
**Lemma II.23.2 (Vernier Conjugate-Pair Lemma, v3.1.7, new, unconditional).** Let $n_3 \geq 2$ be an integer, let $t_1 \in \mathbb{Z}$ and $t_2 \in \mathbb{R}$, and let $r > 0$. Define gearbox indices
$$
k_1 \;=\; n_3 t_1 - t_2, \qquad k_2 \;=\; n_3 t_1 + t_2,
$$
and associated phasors
$$
z_j(s) \;=\; r \cdot e^{i k_j s \pi / n_3}, \qquad j \in \{1, 2\}, \quad s \in \mathbb{R}.
$$
Then at every integer $s$:
$$
z_2(s) \;=\; \overline{z_1(s)}.
$$
The two phasors are exact complex conjugates at every integer signal index.

*Proof.* Compute
$$
z_1(s) \;=\; r \cdot \exp\!\left(i \cdot (n_3 t_1 - t_2) \cdot \frac{s \pi}{n_3}\right) \;=\; r \cdot e^{i s t_1 \pi} \cdot e^{-i s t_2 \pi / n_3}.
$$
At integer $s$ with integer $t_1$, the product $s t_1$ is an integer, so $e^{i s t_1 \pi} = (-1)^{s t_1} \in \{+1, -1\} \subset \mathbb{R}$. Therefore
$$
z_1(s) \;=\; r \cdot (-1)^{s t_1} \cdot e^{-i s t_2 \pi / n_3}.
$$
By identical computation,
$$
z_2(s) \;=\; r \cdot (-1)^{s t_1} \cdot e^{+i s t_2 \pi / n_3} \;=\; \overline{z_1(s)}. \qquad \square
$$

**Corollary II.23.2.1 (Q-residual collapse).** At every integer signal index $s$ with integer $t_1$, the imaginary parts of the gearbox pair sum to zero:
$$
\Im(z_1(s)) + \Im(z_2(s)) \;=\; 0.
$$
More generally, for arbitrary real $s$,
$$
\Im(z_1(s)) + \Im(z_2(s)) \;=\; 2r \sin(s t_1 \pi) \cos(s t_2 \pi / n_3),
$$
which vanishes whenever $s t_1 \in \mathbb{Z}$.

<!-- id: RMK-II.23.2a-RELATION-TO-II.5 | register: remark | status: interpretive | provenance: original | depends: II.5, II.23.2 -->
**Remark II.23.2a (Relation to II.5, v3.1.7, new, interpretive).** Part II / II.5 already states the integer-spin symmetry $\Im(z_1 + z_2) = 0$ for the $(N-1, N+1)$ gearbox — i.e., the $t_1 = t_2 = 1$ reduction — and identifies the $(n_3 t_1 \pm t_2)$ two-parameter family as exposed by FVLT Fourier instrumentation. Lemma II.23.2 generalizes the integer-spin imaginary-part symmetry to the **exact conjugate-pair identity** at all integer $t_1$ and arbitrary real $t_2$, $n_3$, $r$. The $(t_1, t_2)$ range extension to $[-100, +100]$ in `fvlt_fourier_final.html` activates the gearbox-moving factorization-search mode that was previously locked into the small-wedge polygon-trace regime by the $[0, 10]$ range — same formula, expanded resolution.

<!-- id: RMK-II.23.2b-TWO-LAYERS | register: remark | status: interpretive | provenance: original | depends: II.23.2, II.23.3 -->
**Remark II.23.2b (Two layers visible in 3D, one in 2D, v3.1.7, new, interpretive).** The Q-residual collapse (Cor II.23.2.1) is a property of the gearbox formula alone — structurally invariant to the arithmetic content (primality, totient, Möbius value) at the walls $N \pm 1$. It is the **gearbox layer** of the Vernier instrument. Everything that distinguishes defended from leaky configurations (II.23.3) lives at the arithmetic-content layer that rides on top of the gearbox layer. The 2D Vernier projection surfaces only the gearbox layer; the 3D Ramanujan helix renderer surfaces both (II.23.3 geometric reading). The instrument is a differential instrument in the sense of detecting arithmetic-content difference against a guaranteed-conjugate-pair geometric mirror.

<!-- id: COR-II.23.2.2-PAIRED-VERNIER-ALIGNMENT-LOCUS | register: proved | status: unconditional | provenance: original | depends: II.23.2 -->
**Corollary II.23.2.2 (Paired Vernier Alignment Locus).**
In the paired Vernier gearbox
$$z_1 = r\,e^{i(n_3 t_1 - t_2)s\pi/n_3}, \qquad z_2 = r\,e^{i(n_3 t_1 + t_2)s\pi/n_3},$$
with $n_3 > 0$, $r$ real and nonzero, and $t_2 \neq 0$, put $A = t_1 s\pi$ and
$B = t_2 s\pi / n_3$, so that $\theta_1 = A - B$ and $\theta_2 = A + B$. Then:

**(i) (Factorization.)** $\operatorname{Im}(z_1 + z_2) = 2r \sin(A)\cos(B)$.

**(ii) (Exact conjugacy locus.)** $z_2 = \bar z_1 \iff e^{2iA} = 1 \iff t_1 s \in \mathbb{Z}$.
The equivalence uses $r \neq 0$; at $r = 0$ conjugacy holds vacuously
(degenerate disappearance branch, not a structural readout).

**(iii) (Containment.)** Conjugacy implies Q-residual collapse
$\operatorname{Im}(z_1 + z_2) = 0$ — the $\sin$ branch $\sin A = 0$.

**(iv) (Roots-of-unity taxonomy.)** In the nondegenerate scope above,
$$\operatorname{Im}(z_1+z_2)=0 \iff e^{2iA}=1 \ \text{or}\ e^{2iB}=-1.$$
Thus Q-residual collapse is a doubled-phase roots-of-unity test: the alignment
branch hits the trivial root $1$ on the doubled alignment phase and gives
conjugacy; the spread branch hits $\zeta_2=-1$ on the doubled spread phase
and gives cancellation without conjugacy unless it also lies on the alignment
lattice.

**(v) (Properness by witness.)** The containment is proper: the $\cos$ branch
$\cos B = 0$ cancels without pairing. Normalized canonical-parameter witness
$(n_3, t_1, t_2, s, r) = (3, 1, 1, 3/2, 1)$: $\operatorname{Im}(z_1 + z_2) = 0$ while
$|z_2 - \bar z_1| = 2$. More generally, for real nonzero $r$, this witness has
$|z_2 - \bar z_1| = 2|r|$. The witness family persists at the rim:
$(721, 1, 1, 721/2, 1)$ at $n_1 = 6$.

**(vi) (Parity absorption at the canonical reference.)** At $t_1 = t_2 = 1$ the
$\cos$-branch points are $s = n_3(2k+1)/2$, $k \in \mathbb{Z}$. For even $n_3$
these are integers, hence on the alignment lattice: the Q-residual zero locus
and the conjugacy locus coincide on even-$n_3$ slices. For odd $n_3$ they are
half-odd and off-lattice, with $\sin A = \pm 1$: properness is a parity
phenomenon at the canonical reference. In the roots-of-unity language, parity decides
whether the $\zeta_2$ spread-phase hit is absorbed into the $1$ alignment-phase
lattice or remains a genuine off-lattice Q-zero.

*Proof.* $\theta_1 + \theta_2 = 2A$, so $z_2 = \bar z_1 \iff e^{i\theta_2} =
e^{-i\theta_1} \iff e^{i(\theta_1+\theta_2)} = 1 \iff e^{2iA} = 1 \iff
2t_1 s\pi \in 2\pi\mathbb{Z} \iff t_1 s \in \mathbb{Z}$, using $r \neq 0$ to
cancel the modulus. (i) is the product-to-sum identity
$\sin(A-B) + \sin(A+B) = 2\sin A \cos B$. Since $r \neq 0$, (i) vanishes
iff $\sin A = 0$ or $\cos B = 0$; equivalently, $\sin A = 0 \iff e^{2iA}=1$
and $\cos B = 0 \iff e^{2iB}=-1$; this proves (iv). (iii) is immediate
from (i)–(ii). For (v), at $(3,1,1,3/2)$: $A = 3\pi/2$, $B = \pi/2$, so $\cos B = 0$ and
$e^{2iA} = e^{3i\pi} = -1 \neq 1$; and in general $|z_2 - \bar z_1| =
|r|\,|e^{2iA} - 1| = 2|r|\,|\sin A|$, which equals $2|r|$ at every odd-$n_3$
canonical witness since $A$ is there a half-odd multiple of $\pi$. For (vi):
$t_1 s = n_3(2k+1)/2 \in \mathbb{Z} \iff 2 \mid n_3$. $\square$

**Scope relation.** Lemma II.23.2's recorded scope — integer $s$, integer $t_1$,
real $t_2$ — is the specialization of (ii) via $s \in \mathbb{Z},\, t_1 \in
\mathbb{Z} \Rightarrow t_1 s \in \mathbb{Z}$. This corollary promotes the
instrument's docstring condition $t_1 \cdot s \in \mathbb{Z}$ to kernel
strength. It is entered as a **gated scope extension**: the program carried
the stronger statement; the kernel did not, and does not retro-claim it as
having been resident at full strength.

**Register discipline.** This corollary uses the current roots-of-unity register
discipline: $s$, $t_1s$, and $t_2s/n_3$ are numeric / integrality conditions;
$\pi$ appears only as period normalization; no degree measure is introduced.


<!-- id: RMK-II.23.2c-TWO-COLLAPSE-REGISTERS | register: remark | status: unconditional + interpretive | provenance: original | depends: II.23.2, II.23.2.1, II.23.2.2, II.5a, ALG-1 -->
**Remark II.23.2c (Two Collapse Registers).**
The term *collapse* is register-sensitive.

**Q-residual collapse** means the vanishing of $|\operatorname{Im}(z_1 + z_2)|$
in the paired Vernier gearbox. On the conjugate-pair branch this is caused by
the alignment condition $t_1 s \in \mathbb{Z}$ (Cor II.23.2.2(ii)); in the
canonical $t_1 = 1$ reference this is the integer-spin readout. Cancellation
is not identical with conjugacy: the $\cos$ branch $\cos(t_2 s\pi/n_3) = 0$
also gives Q-residual zero without pairing (Cor II.23.2.2(v)), so off-lattice
Q-zeros must not be read as conjugate-pair witnesses. At the canonical
reference these off-lattice Q-zeros occur only at odd $n_3$ (half-odd $s$);
at even $n_3$ the $\cos$ branch is absorbed into the alignment lattice
(Cor II.23.2.2(vi)).

**Divisor-contact readout** is separate: it asks when an individual phasor
lands on the real axis. In the integer scan register this gives
$n_3 \mid t_2 s$; at the canonical reference $t_1 = t_2 = 1$ it specializes
to $n_3 \mid s$, so the readable finite atom is $\mathrm{Div}(s)$
(Rmk II.5a, ALG-1). The integer scan never meets the $\cos$ branch's
off-lattice points; the continuous Q-residual readout does.

Thus before readout, an atom is an $s$-indexed phase potential inside a
bounded factorial shell: $s$ fixes the atom's identity, while
existence-as-readable-set is readout-relative. At $s = 0$, selection vanishes
and the bounded shell returns all admissible $n_3$; at $n_3 \leq 0$ there is
no phasor object — the denominator container is outside the phasor domain;
the instrument's null-return guard implements the exclusion.

### II.23.3 Bracket classification taxonomy

<!-- id: DEF-II.23.3-BRACKET-TAXONOMY | register: definitional | status: definitional | provenance: original | depends: II.23.1 -->
**Definition II.23.3 (Bracket classification, v3.1.7, new).** At any even $N \geq 4$, the trilattice walls $N - 1$ and $N + 1$ are both odd. Define the *bracket class* of the trilattice $(N-1, N, N+1)$ by the primality of the walls:

- **TWIN** — both $N - 1$ and $N + 1$ are prime (defended channel);
- **LEFT_PRIME** — $N - 1$ prime, $N + 1$ composite (leaky bracket, left wall defended);
- **RIGHT_PRIME** — $N + 1$ prime, $N - 1$ composite (leaky bracket, right wall defended);
- **NULL** — neither wall prime (no defense).

The taxonomy is a partition of even $N \geq 4$.

<!-- id: RMK-II.23.3a-MOD-4-WALLS | register: proved | status: unconditional | provenance: original | depends: II.23.3 -->
**Remark II.23.3a (Mod-4 conjugate pairing of walls, v3.1.7, new, unconditional).** For every even $N$, the walls $N - 1$ and $N + 1$ are odd integers differing by $2$, hence sit in opposite mod-$4$ classes:
- If $N \equiv 0 \pmod 4$, then $N - 1 \equiv 3$ and $N + 1 \equiv 1 \pmod 4$.
- If $N \equiv 2 \pmod 4$, then $N - 1 \equiv 1$ and $N + 1 \equiv 3 \pmod 4$.

The mod-$4$ conjugate-pair structure is therefore a property of even $N$ alone, *independent of primality*. The bracket class determines how much of the structure is **populated**: TWIN realizes the mod-$4$ conjugate pair on both sides; LEFT_PRIME / RIGHT_PRIME populates one side only; NULL populates neither.

### II.23.4 Chebyshev-cell interpretation

<!-- id: RMK-II.23.4-CHEBYSHEV-CELL | register: remark | status: interpretive | provenance: original | depends: II.23.3, III.11 -->
**Remark II.23.4 (Chebyshev-cell reading of the bracket taxonomy, v3.1.7, new, interpretive).** Chebyshev's bias is the observation that among small primes, more lie in residue class $3 \pmod 4$ than in class $1 \pmod 4$ — a fact governed by the zeros of the Dirichlet $L$-function $L(s, \chi_4)$, the simplest non-trivial Dirichlet character. The bias is not a fact about primes in any unmediated sense; it is a fact about primes as **read through the specific Fourier instrument at $q = 4$**.

By Rmk II.23.3a, every trilattice at even $N$ has its walls in opposite mod-$4$ classes. For TWIN brackets with $N - 1 \equiv 1, N + 1 \equiv 3 \pmod 4$ (or the reverse), both poles of the $q=4$ Dirichlet decomposition are realized at the local cell $(N-1, N, N+1)$. The bracket taxonomy reads as **localized observational quality of the $q=4$ prime race**:

- **TWIN** = clean Chebyshev observation point (both poles populated, mod-$4$ conjugate pair fully realized);
- **LEFT_PRIME / RIGHT_PRIME** = degraded observation (only one pole populated, conjugate-pair geometry intact but observation single-sided);
- **NULL** = geometry intact, no observation.

The pattern of TWIN / LEFT / RIGHT / NULL brackets along the $N$-axis is the *spatial form of the Chebyshev race itself*: a sequence of localized cells of varying observational quality. The Harmonic Contrast formula (II.23.1.iii) supplies the **quality metric** for each cell — how much room the composite center makes for the prime walls when they exist.

This recasts the framework's role: FVLT does not analyze arithmetic progressions $\{n : n \equiv a \pmod q\}$; it is the geometric form of the instrument that makes them visible. The indicator $\mathbf{1}_{n \equiv a \pmod q}$ for $a$ coprime to $q$ is exactly $(1/\varphi(q)) \sum_\chi \overline{\chi(a)} \chi(n)$ — a sum of Dirichlet characters. Set-theoretically the residue classes exist regardless; analytically, they become visible to counting only through the Fourier representation. The kernel's character / supercharacter machinery (II.2, II.8) is the same kind of Fourier instrument operating at fixed finite $N$. $\mathrm{Div}(720)$ is what the Fourier instrument reads when asked what arithmetic progressions are visible at this resolution.

### II.23.5 Goldbach–Arnold-tongue layer

<!-- id: RMK-II.23.5-ARNOLD-TONGUE | register: remark | status: interpretive | provenance: original | depends: III.11, II.23.2 -->
**Remark II.23.5 (Arnold-tongue reading of Goldbach pairs, v3.1.7, new, interpretive).** Part III / III.11 supplies the resonant-pair structure: for even $N \geq 4$, each Goldbach pair $\{p, q\}$ with $p + q = N$ gives a coherent pair of Ramanujan channels (Thm 6.2). At the measurement-instrument level, each such pair is **modeled as seeding a phase-locked region**: the rational point $p/N \in [0, 1]$ surrounded by a tongue of approximate width $1/\sqrt{pq}$ (the geometric mean of channel widths), within which signal phases lock to the $(p, q)$ pair.

This is the **Arnold-tongue reading** of the Goldbach atlas. In the dynamical systems literature, Arnold tongues are phase-locked regions in parameter space; here the parameter is rational $p/N$ and the tongues are seeded by Goldbach pairs. Tongue occupancy gives an operational, interpretive classification of data:

- A signal whose energy concentrates in one tongue is locked to a specific Goldbach pair.
- A signal whose energy spreads across many tongues is broadly Goldbach-coherent.
- A signal whose energy avoids tongues is Goldbach-incoherent at the operating shell.

The Arnold-tongue reading is interpretive — it is a measurement-level perception that bridges III.11's substrate-level resonant-pair definition to the operational scoring of data. The width $1/\sqrt{pq}$ is a model estimate (geometric mean of $1/p$ and $1/q$ channel widths from II.8); a sharper width analysis is open. See open problems, Part VII / VII.7.

<!-- id: RMK-II.23.5a-GOLDBACH-COMPLEMENT-AS-CONJUGATION | register: remark | status: unconditional (orbit identity) + conditional-on-Goldbach (prime fiber) | provenance: adapted (classical cyclotomic conjugation identity) over original (FVLT seating, (N,1,p,2) specialization, sigma_{-1}-orbit reading, prime-filter split) | depends: II.23.2, II.23.3a, II.23.5, III.11, 9.3b -->
**Remark II.23.5a (Goldbach complement as cyclotomic conjugation; prime-selected σ₋₁-orbits in μ_N, new).**

Let $N \ge 2$, let $\zeta_N = e^{2\pi i/N}$, and let $C_N : t \mapsto N - t$ be the additive-complement involution on $\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}$. Then
$$
\zeta_N^{\,C_N(t)} \;=\; \zeta_N^{-t} \;=\; \overline{\zeta_N^{\,t}}.
$$
Additive complement on exponents is complex conjugation on the phase register — equivalently the Galois action $\sigma_{-1} \in \mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_N)/\mathbb{Q})$, $\zeta_N \mapsto \zeta_N^{-1}$. Among Galois actions, $\sigma_{-1}$ is the *distinguished* operation native to both the additive register ($\mathbb{Z}/N$) and the multiplicative register ($\mu_N$) for the complement relation; it is nontrivial iff $N > 2$.

Consequently every decomposition $p + q = N$ determines the $\sigma_{-1}$-orbit
$$
\{\zeta_N^{\,p},\, \zeta_N^{\,q}\} \;=\; \{\zeta_N^{\,p},\, \zeta_N^{-p}\} \;\subset\; \mu_N .
$$
A Goldbach pair is **not an atlas edge**; it is a $\sigma_{-1}$-orbit whose two exponent representatives survive the prime filter $\{\,p : p\text{ prime and } N - p \text{ prime}\,\}$. The orbit geometry is unconditional and prime-blind; the Goldbach depth lives entirely in the selection, not in the geometry. The Arnold-tongue layer (Rmk II.23.5) may decorate the surviving orbits but does not establish their existence or density.

**Seat within Lemma II.23.2.** This is the Vernier Conjugate-Pair Lemma at $(n_3, t_1, t_2, s) = (N, 1, p, 2)$, read on $\mu_N$. The $(N, 1, p)$ gearbox has indices $k_1 = N - p = q$, $k_2 = N + p$; at $s = 2$ the pair evaluates to $\{\zeta_N^{\,q}, \zeta_N^{\,p}\}$, since $N + p \equiv p \pmod N$. The $s = 2$ seat is unconditional and prime-blind. By contrast, the $s = 1$ half-turn slice is *not* a universal exclusion: it coincides with the same $\mu_N$-orbit exactly on the locus $p = N/3$ (when $3 \mid N$). That coincidence never survives the Goldbach prime filter, since then $q = 2N/3 = 2 \cdot (N/3)$ is even and composite. Thus on the prime-surviving Goldbach fiber, $s = 1$ does not read the pair, while $s = 2$ does. The alignment locus $t_1 s \in \mathbb{Z}$ (Cor II.23.2.2(ii)) places every integer $s$ — $s = 2$ included — on the alignment lattice, so the orbit is a genuine conjugate-pair *alignment-branch* witness, never a $\cos$-branch off-lattice Q-zero. It satisfies the Rmk II.23.2c guard by construction.

**Unification with the wall taxonomy.** II.23.2 / II.23.3a pairs *straddle* $N$ as $(N \mp p)$; Goldbach pairs *sum* to $N$ as $(p, N - p)$. On $\mu_N$ these are the same two roots of unity. The gearbox-wall reading (walls $N \pm 1$, and the moving gearbox generally) and the interior-complement reading are one relation reduced mod $N$.

**Orbit size and the $-1$ bead.** The orbit is genuinely two-element iff $N \nmid 2p$, i.e. $p \not\equiv 0,\, N/2$. The only fixed point is the degenerate chord $p = q = N/2$: the antipodal bead $-1 = \zeta_N^{\,N/2}$, which exists iff $N$ is even (Rmk 9.3b, parity of cyclic lift). It is prime-selected only when $N/2$ is prime, i.e. $N = 2 \cdot (\text{prime})$. The action $\sigma_{-1}$ exists for every $N$; the $-1$ bead does not.

**Trace (metric shadow).** The real pair-sum is $\zeta_N^{\,p} + \zeta_N^{-p} = 2\cos(2\pi p/N)$, the trace of $\sigma_{-1}$ on the orbit. This is a metric magnitude — a display shadow, not the chord coordinate. The chord $z \to \bar z$ has constant real part and is *vertical* in the standard complex plane; any horizontal rendering is an axis/display convention. The midpoint real coordinate is $\cos(2\pi p/N)$.

**Status.** Orbit identity: **unconditional** (specialization of the unconditional Lemma II.23.2). Prime-surviving fiber: **conditional on Goldbach**, inheriting Thm 6.3 — existence/coverage of prime-surviving orbits is the conditional part. Tongue-width sharpening for the surviving orbits ($1/\sqrt{pq}$ model, Rmk II.23.5) remains **open** (Part VII / VII.7).

> *The additive complement is cyclotomic conjugation; Goldbach pairs are the prime-surviving $\sigma_{-1}$-orbits.*

**Register discipline.** Phases are roots of unity; $\sigma_{-1} : \zeta_N \mapsto \zeta_N^{-1}$ is the object, $2\cos(2\pi p/N)$ its metric trace. $\pi$ appears only as period normalization; no degree measure is introduced.

<!-- id: RMK-II.23.5b-GALOIS-CONNECTIONS-MEET-AT-COMPLEMENT | register: remark | status: unconditional (complement/conjugation) + definitional (prime filter) + conditional-on-Goldbach (coverage) + open (density) | provenance: original | depends: II.23.5a, 1.5b, 9.7q, 9.7n, 9.3b, III.11 -->
**Remark II.23.5b (Galois connections meet at complement; Goldbach begins at prime survival, new).**

The divisor atlas, FCA concept lattices, and the field-theoretic Galois correspondence are three faces of one order-theoretic pattern: an antitone passage between two ordered registers, with closure appearing in the composites and the fixed/closed objects forming a lattice. The atlas is recovered as a concept lattice through the closure-of-description Galois connection (Rmk 1.5b, canonical recovery via Birkhoff [Bir40]); $\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_N)/\mathbb{Q}$ carries the subgroup$\leftrightarrow$subfield correspondence with Galois group $(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^\times$.

For the cyclotomic phase register, the additive-complement involution
$$
C_N : t \mapsto N - t
$$
is transported to $\zeta_N^{\,t} \mapsto \zeta_N^{-t}$, namely the Galois action $\sigma_{-1}$ (II.23.5a). The subgroup $\langle \sigma_{-1} \rangle$ fixes the maximal real subfield
$$
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_N)^+ = \mathbb{Q}\!\left(\zeta_N + \zeta_N^{-1}\right).
$$
A conjugate pair is **not** a fixed object of the correspondence — the fixed object is the field $\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_N)^+$. The pair $\{\zeta_N^{\,p}, \zeta_N^{-p}\}$ is $\langle\sigma_{-1}\rangle$-*stable*, and its real orbit-sum $\zeta_N^{\,p} + \zeta_N^{-p} = 2\cos(2\pi p/N)$ *lies in* the fixed field — the same real-subfield home as the integer-spin Vernier readout (ALG-2) and the apex defect (Lem 9.7q). This seats the Goldbach chord geometry exactly: the additive complement is cyclotomic conjugation.

The geometry is closed under the Galois action; the prime filter is not. Prime-survival is a predicate on the integer representatives $p$ and $N - p$, not a property detected by the cyclotomic field lattice, the divisor lattice of $N$, or the $\sigma_{-1}$-orbit closure. Apart from the degenerate pair $p = q = N/2$ — where $p \mid N$, the antipodal bead $-1 = \zeta_N^{\,N/2}$ (Rmk 9.3b) — Goldbach primes are units mod $N$, and the cyclotomic machine treats prime and composite unit exponents as the same species of phase object.

The wall is native-closure-specific, and must be stated as such: FCA is universal — every finite lattice is a concept lattice — so one can always build an artificial formal context carrying a "prime-survives" attribute whose concept lattice trivially sees the filter. The claim is therefore **not** that no closure sees prime-survival, but that

> no closure operator native to the divisor atlas, the cyclotomic field correspondence, or the $\sigma_{-1}$ phase action detects prime-survival without importing primality as an external predicate.

This is the native-closure discipline of Note 9.7n applied to the additive register: closures are unconditional inside their arithmetic substrate, and the primality predicate is outside all three substrates in play here.

Therefore FCA and the Galois correspondence hand over the complement geometry unconditionally. Goldbach begins precisely where that native closure stops: at the requirement that both additive complements land in the externally selected set of primes.

**Status.** Complement/conjugation identity: **unconditional**. Prime-selected fiber: **definitional**. Global nonempty coverage for all even $N$: **conditional on Goldbach** (III.11 / Thm 6.3). Density / tongue-width sharpening: **open**.

**Register note.** Three $\sigma$-glyphs meet at this seat and stay distinct (cf. v3.1.20): the Galois automorphism $\sigma_{-1}$ (this remark), the exponent signature $\sigma(n)$, and the FCA derivation-predicate $\sigma$ (Rmk 1.5b). The boundary is exactly the refusal to let the additive complement register and the multiplicative primality register collapse into one closure.

> *Closure reaches conjugation, not Goldbach.*

### II.23.6 L/D signature measurement protocol

<!-- id: PROTO-II.23.6-LD-SIGNATURE | register: empirical | status: empirical | provenance: original | depends: II.23.1 -->
**Protocol II.23.6 (L/D signature, v3.1.7, new, empirical).** For a signal $x$ traced through the FVLT instrument at shell $N$, define:

- **Arc length** $L(x)$ — total path length of the trajectory in the supercharacter / Ramanujan channel space (or its PCA / phase-space projection, viewer-mode dependent);
- **Diameter** $D(x)$ — maximum pairwise distance between trajectory points in the same projection;
- **L/D ratio** — the dimensionless quantity $L(x) / D(x)$.

L/D measures **winding density**: high L/D = trajectory winds densely within a confined region; low L/D = trajectory makes a broad sweep with little winding.

**Empirical result (single $n=2$ measurement, May 19–20, 2026).** Two signals run through the Universal Analyzer at $v10$ at $N = 720$ with OHLCV encoding gave:

| Signal | Source | Arc length $L$ | Diameter $D$ | $L/D$ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HLA region | `chr6_hla_market.csv`, 500 rows | $8.38 \times 10^4$ | $2.45$ | $\approx 3.4 \times 10^4$ |
| PLTR | `pltr_us.csv`, 230 rows | $4.28 \times 10^4$ | $200.9$ | $\approx 213$ |

The L/D ratio separates by **two orders of magnitude** between the two signal classes. Both $\gamma \approx 0.04$ (similar low spine adherence); both $H_T \approx 0.63$ (similar trilattice entropy). The L/D ratio is the cleanest single-number separator visible across the present panels of measurement primitives.

**Structural interpretation (single-instance, subject to validation conditions below, interpretive / open).**
- *Genomic-like* signal (HLA): narrow and long — extreme winding within a confined region. Locks to a μ-class wall (here $c_{719}$ at $81.4\%$), wanders densely within it.
- *Capital-like* signal (PLTR): wide and shorter — broad exploration with less winding within local channels. Escapes channel-locking, wanders sparsely across phase space.

**Validation conditions (required for promotion from empirical to structural).** The L/D signature is an empirical observation from a single $n=2$ measurement at fixed shell and viewer mode. Promotion requires:

1. **Lattice variation.** Replicate at additional shells, especially defended-channel $N$ values ($N = 30$ with HC $= 3.625$, $N = 42$ with HC $= 3.0$, primorial centers) and leaky-bracket $N$ values ($N = 38$ with HC $= 5/3$); verify the separation persists or sharpens at defended shells.
2. **Within-class replication.** Multiple genomic regions (not only HLA); multiple equity-like instruments (not only PLTR); multiple time windows for each.
3. **Encoding sensitivity.** OHLCV is one of several possible signal encodings; verify the L/D separation is encoding-robust or characterize its encoding dependence.
4. **Projection sensitivity.** The $L$ and $D$ values depend on the trajectory projection (PCA, phase-space, channel-magnitude). Verify the separation persists across the analyzer's standard projections or characterize its projection dependence.
5. **Null calibration.** Apply the protocol to shuffled / phase-randomized surrogates that preserve marginal statistics but destroy structure (cf. Rmk 8.20e.1, Part V / V.17). The L/D signature on surrogates supplies the noise floor against which the HLA-vs-PLTR separation must be measured.

Until these conditions are met, the L/D signature is reported as an **empirical observation with validation conditions stated in prose**, not as a kernel claim about life-vs-capital signal classes. The $n=2$ measurement is reportable; the structural reading is **interpretive / open** pending validation.

### II.23.7 Adaptive variance-driven operating shell

**Source:** Ratioque corporate integrity monitor v2.6 (Robin Miller, November 30, 2025); Axiom A2 "Weyl-Miller Gauge Potential" — formula preserved, framing recast via Rmk 7.1.
**v3.0 location:** Part II / II.23.7.
**Status:** empirical; interpretive structural reading stated.

<!-- id: RMK-II.23.7-ADAPTIVE-SHELL | register: remark | status: empirical | provenance: original | depends: 7.1, II.23.2, IV.1 -->
**Remark II.23.7 (Log-Compressed Adaptive Operating Shell, v3.1.10, new, empirical).** For Vernier-instrument deployments where the operating shell $n_3$ adapts to signal variance, the Ratioque v2.6 canonical schedule is:
$$
n_3(t) \;=\; N_{\mathrm{base}} \cdot \bigl(1 + G(t)\bigr), \qquad G(t) \;=\; \ln\bigl(1 + k \nu(t)\bigr),
$$
where $\nu(t)$ is the rolling sample variance of an instrument-declared signal channel over an instrument-declared window, $k > 0$ is the gauge-stiffness parameter, and $N_{\mathrm{base}}$ is the canonical instrument shell (typically $720$).

**Weyl-1918 gauge reading (Rmk 7.1).** $G(t)$ is the Weyl-1918 *scalar scale potential* (the log-scale coordinate; the associated *connection one-form* is $A = dG$; see Rmk 7.1) of the local scale factor $\lambda(t) = 1 + k\nu(t)$, with the breathing shell carried as
$$
n_3(t) \;=\; N_{\mathrm{base}} \cdot \bigl(1 + \log \lambda(t)\bigr).
$$
The "$1 +$" prefix on the scale factor is the *small-$G$ linearization* $1 + G \approx e^G$ of the Weyl exponential scale-change. The linearization is what the validity domain below bounds.

**Properties.**
- Anchored at zero variance: $G(0) = 0$, so $n_3(0) = N_{\mathrm{base}}$ (the shell collapses to base in the absence of signal).
- Finite slope at zero: $dG/d\nu \big|_{\nu = 0} = k$, no zero-variance singularity (unlike $\sqrt{\nu}$ or $\log \nu$).
- Sub-linear growth: $G(\nu) \sim \ln(k\nu)$ as $\nu \to \infty$, so heavy-tailed variance does not blow up the operating shell unboundedly.
- Compatibility with II.23.2: $n_3(t)$ positive real; the algebraic extension of II.23.2's proof (Rmk 7.1) applies, so the conjugate-pair identity $z_2 = \bar z_1$ at integer $s$ holds at every $t$.

**Validity domain (empirical).** Calibrated for $k \in [1.5, 3.0]$ on financial-signal data (rolling 14-day daily-return standard deviation as $\sqrt{\nu}$; $k = 2$ canonical for that calibration range). Deployed in the Ratioque v2.6 corporate integrity monitor and run on four datasets (SPY 2020 COVID, SPY 2025 correction, DJI 2025 healthy, PLTR 2025 hypergrowth) with state-distribution and asset-class classification consistent across regimes. The structural reading of those validations is *interpretive / open* — the empirical performance is documented, the structural mechanism that explains it is not yet derived.

**Sensitivity caveat and $G_{\max}$ cap.** Vernier sensitivity (II.23 framework) scales as $\delta(\mathrm{Shear}) \sim 2/n_3$, so a sufficiently large $n_3$ degrades the conjugate-pair signal. An instrument-tuning cap $G_{\max}$ (e.g., $G \leq 1$, giving $n_3 \leq 2 N_{\mathrm{base}}$) is recommended for deployments where Vernier sensitivity at high variance is load-bearing. The cap is also where the small-$G$ linearization $1 + G \approx e^G$ breaks down: at $G = 1$ the linearization has $\approx 26.4\%$ relative error versus the exponential ($1 + 1 = 2$ vs $e^1 \approx 2.718$). The cap is therefore the structural boundary of the linearization, not an arbitrary fudge.

**Why this functional form.** The choice $\lambda(t) = 1 + k\nu(t)$ is one Weyl-1918 modeling option; alternatives include $\lambda(t) = e^{k\nu(t)}$ (pure exponential, no linearization), $\lambda(t) = (1 + k\nu(t))^p$ (polynomial), or sigmoid forms. The $1 + k\nu$ choice was made empirically. No structural derivation from kernel content selects it uniquely.

**Provenance.** Ratioque v2.6 corporate integrity monitor (Robin Miller, November 30, 2025), developed through Robin + Gemini exploration of Weyl gauge theory (fall 2025). The empirical schedule survives v2.6 → v3.1.x sharpening; the original v2.6 framing (outer-automorphism-space, Monster representation activation) is *superseded* by v3.1.9 Out($S_6$) closure (Rmk 8.20h.5) and held to bibliography OP5 (Langlands speculation) territory.

### II.23.8 Period-q phase coherence and phase-break localization

<!-- id: RMK-II.23.8-PHASE-COHERENCE-LOCALIZATION | register: remark | status: interpretive | provenance: original | depends: II.23.1, 5.3, DEF-9.7o-WINDING-ONLY-REVERSAL, II.6, ALG-1, ALG-2, ALG-3, 9.7a, 9.3a.1, 9.7h -->
**Remark II.23.8 (Period-q Phase Coherence and Phase-Break Localization, v3.1.19, new, interpretive).** Let $\langle x, c_q \rangle_w$ denote the §5.3 complex-valued channel coefficient applied to the restriction of a signal $x$ to a local window $w$ at period $q \mid N$ — the complex coefficient §5.3 distinguishes from its energy $|\langle x, c_q \rangle|^2$, and distinct from the real supercharacter readout $c_q(x)$ of Def II.23.1, which discards phase. Two readouts ride on $\langle x, c_q \rangle_w$:

- **Presence (magnitude).** $|\langle x, c_q \rangle_w|$ reads the presence of $q$-periodic structure in the window — the magnitude side, as in the existing measurement primitives (Def II.23.1, Protocol II.23.6).
- **Phase coherence and break (argument).** $\arg \langle x, c_q \rangle_w$ reads the phase of that structure. *Phase coherence* — $\arg \langle x, c_q \rangle_w$ stable across adjacent windows up to the slowly varying envelope — indicates a single stable $q$-phase ("$q$-frame"). A localized relative step $\langle x, c_q \rangle_{w^+} / \langle x, c_q \rangle_{w^-} \approx \zeta_q^k$ between two coherent regions localizes a **phase break** of $k$ steps in the $q$-cycle.

**Register (v3.1.18).** The phase readout is a roots-of-unity statement first: the break is multiplication by the root of unity $\zeta_q^k$, not a degree value; a displayed "$120^\circ$ jump" at $q = 3$ is the decimal shadow of the $\zeta_3$ step (roots-of-unity register discipline, front matter). The magnitude $|\langle x, c_q \rangle_w|$ is a metric quantity outside the roots-of-unity register. The *absolute* phase class is reference-dependent — $\arg$ is defined against an origin — so the origin-free content of this primitive is (i) coherence and (ii) the relative $\zeta_q^k$ step; naming the absolute $q$-phase requires an external reference.

**Relation to the phasor-side algorithmic core.** This primitive is the measurement-side complement of the single-phasor generative pass. ALG-1 / ALG-2 / ALG-3 read the Fourier phasor with no ingested signal: ALG-1 takes contact / zero events as the constructive Hasse-vertex readout, ALG-2 retains the fuller $\gcd(s,N)$ cyclotomic stratification carried by off-contact samples, and ALG-3 reads continuous winding between divisor nodes as lattice ratios. II.23.8 reads an ingested signal through the resulting channel coordinate system: Def II.23.1 gives the phase-blind real / magnitude-side measurement, while II.23.8 keeps the relative $\zeta_q^k$ phase. In this sense
$$
\text{II.23.8} : \text{Def II.23.1} \;::\; \text{ALG-2} : \text{ALG-1},
$$
not as an identity of quantities, but as the same narrow-readout / fuller-winding-readout relation on opposite arrows: construction of the coordinate system versus measurement through it.

**Four readouts of one cyclotomic winding.** The framework therefore distinguishes four compatible readouts of the same cyclotomic winding architecture: (i) **generative** — ALG-1/2/3 construct vertices, strata, and ratios from the phasor-side instrument; (ii) **geometric** — the §9.7 helix / embedding freezes that winding as lattice coordinates (each per-prime fiber is the chord polygon of $\Gamma_{n_i}$ from the §9.1a concentric form, identical up to translation — Lemma 9.7a, Note 9.3a.1 — and the atom is their Minkowski sum, Rmk 9.7h); (iii) **harmonic, phase-blind** — the orbit-summed real supercharacter $c_q$ and the magnitude / energy primitives discard phase; and (iv) **harmonic, phase-retaining** — the complex coefficient $\langle x, c_q \rangle$ retains argument for signal measurement. These are register-separated readouts of one cyclotomic architecture, not four unrelated mechanisms.

**Distinction from Cl(s) (Part II / II.6).** The clinamen $\mathrm{Cl}(s)$ is the per-step phase deviation of the FVLT phasor curve itself (the instrument's own geometry). This primitive is the windowed period-$q$ phase of an *ingested signal's* channel coefficient. The two are cyclotomically cognate — they live against the same phasor-side / roots-of-unity scaffolding — but they are not the same number. Constructor phase and measurement phase must not be conflated.

**Status.** Measurement primitive, not a theorem. It instantiates (a) the magnitude/phase split of the harmonic readout already named at §5.3 (complex $\langle x, c_q \rangle$ vs energy $|\langle x, c_q \rangle|^2$), and (b) the Lem 9.7o complementarity that winding-side readouts retain structure scalar readouts can average away. Empirical instance (recorded as measurement outcome, not kernel claim): a period-3 GC/AT-component frame test on real GAPDH coding sequence read phase coherence $R = 0.91$ (coding) vs $R = 0.13$ (introns) and localized an artificial one-base deletion as a single $\zeta_3$ relative step while the channel magnitude stayed flat across the cut — frame change, origin-free.
