<!-- id: RMK-VI.3a-GOLDBACH-PAIRS-AS-TUPLE-SEEDS | register: remark | status: unconditional (complement geometry) + measurement-tier (survival prediction) + application-tier (tuple seeds) + not-claimed (coverage) | provenance: original | depends: II.23.5a, II.23.5b, VI.3, II.23.5, III.11 -->
**Remark VI.3a (Goldbach pairs as tuple seeds, not theorem targets; research-track / measurement-tier, new).**

The Goldbach layer is not used here to prove Goldbach. For each even $N$ the additive-complement involution $p \mapsto N - p$ seats candidate pairs as $\sigma_{-1}$-orbits in the cyclotomic phase register (II.23.5a); the geometry is unconditional and prime-blind (II.23.5b). The algorithmic task is not to establish that every even $N$ contains a prime-surviving orbit, but to **score, rank, or predict** which conjugate orbits survive the external prime filter $p \in \mathbb{P},\; N - p \in \mathbb{P}$. The instrument is a measurement-tier classifier over $\sigma_{-1}$-orbits of $\mu_N$, with Goldbach pairs used as tuple seeds rather than as a theorem endpoint.

**Centered-tuple coordinate.** A Goldbach pair is a centered prime 2-tuple about $N/2$:
$$
p = N/2 - a, \qquad q = N/2 + a.
$$
The offset $a$ is the tuple coordinate; the $\sigma_{-1}$-orbit supplies the symmetry; prime survival supplies the measured selection. Higher tuple work is then coupled offsets $(N/2 - a_i,\; N/2 + a_i)$ or one-sided combs $N/2 + a_1, \ldots, N/2 + a_k$ — calibrated multi-prime seeds built on the two-body symmetry.

**Audit protocol (pre-registration of the empirical gate).**
1. *Ranking* — for each $N$, do true pairs appear near the top of the predicted orbit list?
2. *Calibration* — when the classifier assigns score $s$, does survival frequency match $s$?
3. *Tuple lift* — do high-scoring offsets $a$ also improve discovery of larger prime-tuple patterns?
4. *Ablation (granularity-split)* — remove geometric feature families and measure degradation, **separating two granularities**:
   - *gcd-signature-coarse* features (bare gcd-signature $\gcd(p,N)$ of the offset). **Predicted null.** Every coprime candidate offset shares gcd-signature $\{1\}$ — verified constant across $N = 720,\, 30030,\, 720720$ — so the gcd-signature carries zero ranking power over offsets. (The conductor of a unit exponent is full $N$, not $1$; it too is constant across coprime offsets and carries no ranking power.) Ablating it degrades nothing; that is coarseness, not the geometry failing.
   - *Residue-fine* features ($p \bmod$ the small primes; the covering-system / singular-series admissibility that II.23.5's $1/\sqrt{pq}$ model already gestures at). **The actual test.** This is where survival signal lives — the survivor distribution is visibly non-uniform across residues (e.g. mod $7$, survivors do not spread evenly). If ablating the residue-fine family degrades prediction, that feature family is load-bearing inside the orbit-organized search space; if not, the current classifier has not shown contribution beyond generic residue/primality heuristics.

The granularity-split is the load-bearing part of the protocol: without it, question 4 returns a foregone null and would falsely convict the geometry of doing no work, when the real fault would be feeding the classifier the wrong-granularity features.

**Status.** Complement geometry: **unconditional** (II.23.5a/b). Prime-surviving orbit prediction: **measurement-tier** (empirical/computational, gated by the protocol above). Goldbach pairs as centered tuple seeds: **application-tier**. Proof of Goldbach coverage: **not claimed**.

**Firewall (standing).** The residue-fine features are, classically, the singular-series admissibility data. The content of this remark is the *organization* of the conjugate-orbit search space by the phase register, not a number-theoretic advance in prime-counting; no primality, factoring, or coverage claim is made. A classifier that passes the four-question protocol would seat later as an ALG note with its own verifier (parallel to ALG-5 / VII.2); this remark pre-registers the gate, it does not claim a passing instrument.

> *The instrument organizes the conjugate-orbit search space and identifies prime-surviving fibers; it does not prove they are always there.*

## VI.6 Algorithmic speedups

**Source:** §10.1–§10.7 (v2.7.14).  
**v3.0 location:** Part VI / VI.6.  
**Status:** per §10 block-level.

**§10.1 Reflection Symmetry (preserved).** $c_q(s) = c_q(q - s)$ — exploit for halved channel computation.

**§10.2 Direct Neighbor Computation (preserved).** Hasse-cover neighbors computable in $O(m)$ from prime-exponent vector — no full traversal needed.

**§10.3 Stern–Brocot Tree Navigation (preserved).** Farey-neighbor queries via Stern–Brocot tree at $O(\log n)$ — replaces $O(n^2)$ pairwise mediant enumeration. Cross-reference to Part II / II.13 helix readout for Farey-symmetric foundations.

**§10.4 Tree Pruning for Targeted Analysis (preserved).** Branch-and-bound pruning of Stern–Brocot subtrees when channel-energy thresholds are known.

**§10.5 Combined Speedups (preserved).** Composing §10.1–§10.4 yields $O(\tau(N) \cdot \log(\Omega_{\max}))$ total cost for a single $E_q$ scan — far less than naive $O(N \log N)$.

**§10.6 Torus Walk Optimization (preserved).** CRT-product torus structure enables $O(\prod r_i)$ walk steps in fibered representation rather than $O(\tau(N))$ in flat representation.

**§10.7 CRT Duality Speedups (preserved).** CRT factorization $c_{AB}(s) = c_A(s) \cdot c_B(s)$ (Thm 8.19) reduces fused-lens computations to product of basis and complement computations — $O(|B(720)|) + O(|B(1001)|) = O(30) + O(8)$ instead of $O(240)$.

**§10.8 Atlas-Level Symmetry Speedup: Profile-Class Transport (v3.1.5, new).** Speedups §10.1–§10.7 are *atom-internal*: each reduces the cost of a computation on a single atom $\mathrm{Div}(N)$. Their two-stage structure — product-structure collapse (§10.6, §10.7; Thm K.2) followed by permutation-symmetry collapse (§7 gauge group; §5.1a orbit coarsening) — is the atom-internal picture. §10.8 carries that picture to atlas scale through the recursion hinge of Part I / I.8: the atlas is itself a divisibility-ordered lattice, and coprime atoms compose as a direct product (Rmk 2.2e).

**(a) The signature-shape axis is a computational-cost axis.** *[interpretive]* The Part III / III.1 signature-shape classification (constant / strictly-decreasing / general-weakly-decreasing / non-monotone) and the closed/strained partition (Rmk 9.7e; III.2–III.3) sort atoms by geometric strain. They equally sort atoms by symmetry-collapse depth. A constant signature gives the full $S_m$ automorphism, so a separable equivariant quantity computes from one representative per $S_m$-orbit of exponent vectors — in the squarefree (Boolean) sub-family these orbits are the $\Omega$-rank levels with binomial sizes $\binom{m}{j}$. A weakly-decreasing signature gives the Young subgroup $\prod_i S_{m_i}$, so the computation collapses to one representative per *distinct* exponent. An atom's symmetry-speedup class is therefore fixed by its position on the III.1 signature-shape axis and is readable from $\sigma(N)$ before any computation runs. This is the Note 9.7m position-awareness property (Part II / II.18) lifted one level — from intra-atom position to inter-atom cost class.

**(b) Profile-class transport.** *[unconditional core; transport reading operational]* Define the *profile class* of an atom $\mathrm{Div}(N)$ as the multiset $\{a_i\}$ of its exponent signature $\sigma(N)$. By unique factorization and Birkhoff duality (Aut of a finite distributive lattice $=$ Aut of its poset of join-irreducibles; for $\mathrm{Div}(N)$ the join-irreducibles are the prime-power chains), **any two atoms in the same profile class have isomorphic divisor lattices** — hence identical automorphism group $\prod_i S_{m_i}$, identical Thm K.2 Cartesian-product structure, identical closed/strained status, identical apex defect $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}$, **and identical ambient cyclotomic field-of-definition bound $L = \mathrm{lcm}(m, n_0, \ldots, n_{m-1})$ for $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}$ (Lemma 9.7q, Part II / II.15)**. *Profile class $=$ isomorphism class of atoms.* Rmk 9.7f records the single instance $\mathrm{Div}(30) \cong \mathrm{Div}(1001)$ (shared profile $(1,1,1)$); §10.8(b) is that statement made atlas-general.

Consequently any **profile-register quantity** — one depending on $\sigma(N)$ only up to prime relabeling — is constant across its entire profile class: it is computed once and *transported* to every other atom in the class. Each profile class contains infinitely many atoms (one per choice of $m$ distinct primes realizing the profile). Where §10.1–§10.7 save work *within* one computation, profile-class transport removes the computation entirely for every atom past the first in its class.

This is a second, distinct group action. The automorphism group $\prod_i S_{m_i}$ acts *within* an atom by permuting equal-exponent primes (the action exploited by §7 and §10.1–§10.7). The profile-class relation acts *across* the atlas, relating distinct atoms of the same signature shape. §10.8 exploits the second action; it is the atlas-native counterpart of the atom-internal speedup.

**(c) Transport is profile-register-bounded.** *[register-discipline; established by C3e — pointer, not derivation. Failure-boundary wording corrected at the v3.1.5 commit — sharper than, and replacing, the earlier Chris-audited draft wording; the replacement is itself Chris-audited (PASS).]* Profile-class transport is valid **exactly on the profile register** — quantities invariant under prime relabeling, i.e. determined by the exponent multiset $\{a_i\}$ alone — and fails on every register finer than that: the **prime-rank register** (Note C3e-II.1, Part II / II.18), partition, prime, bridge, harmonic/channel. The prime-rank register is the easily-missed one: a profile class mixes rank assignments — $\mathrm{Div}(2^4 \cdot 3^2 \cdot 5)$ and $\mathrm{Div}(3^4 \cdot 2^2 \cdot 5)$ both lie in profile class $(4,2,1)$ (see *Concrete instances* below) yet assign the largest exponent to a different-ranked prime — so a quantity sensitive to exponent-by-prime-size order does not transport even though it names no prime. Transport carries the relabeling-invariant layer and nothing finer. Two atoms in one profile class share their lattice, automorphism group $\prod_i S_{m_i}$, closed/strained status, apex defect $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}$, and profile-register cost class; they do not share prime-rank assignment, basis/complement membership, prime identities, Ramanujan channels, bridge diagnostics, or harmonic readouts unless a separate invariant proves it. Concretely, $\mathrm{Div}(30)$ and $\mathrm{Div}(1001)$ are both Boolean $B_3$ — identical lattice, identical automorphism group — yet carry channels $\{2,3,5\}$ versus $\{7,11,13\}$, so their $c_q$ / $E_q$ / bridge readouts do not transport. This is the C3e result — prime-register structure is absent from the unweighted divisor graph — read as a scope condition on §10.8: *the speedup map and the register map coincide.* The non-transport is not a limitation of §10.8; it is the content of C3e.

**Instrument consequence.** *[unconditional — composition of §7, Rmk 2.2e, and (b)]* The 720/1001 bridge instance decomposes as $\mathrm{Div}(720720) = \mathrm{Div}(720) \times \mathrm{Div}(1001)$ (Rmk 2.2e; V.4). The basis factor $\mathrm{Div}(720)$, signature $(4,2,1)$, is strictly-decreasing — lattice-rigid, automorphism group trivial on basis primes (§7), zero Stage-2 collapse. The complement factor $\mathrm{Div}(1001)$, profile $(1,1,1)$, is Boolean — full $S_3$, maximal Stage-2 collapse. §10.7 already factors a fused-lens computation across the CRT split; §10.8 adds that the two factors occupy different signature-shape classes, so the instrument's **permutation-symmetry speedup is complement-loaded**: the entire Stage-2 (orbit-collapse) saving lives on the complement cube, and the basis body pays the full strained per-factor cost. The Stage-1 (product-structure) speedup, by contrast, applies symmetrically to both factors.

**Scaling — the superprimorial witness.** *[unconditional; canonical-witness framing interpretive]* The Stage-1 product-structure collapse (§10.6, §10.7) is not a constant-factor win — its margin grows without bound along the superprimorial family (Rmk 9.7l; Part III / III.7). For $\mathrm{Sp}_n$ the distinct-prime count $m = n$ grows *linearly* while the node count $\tau(\mathrm{Sp}_n) = (n+1)!$ grows *factorially*. Per-strand (Stage-1) work is the strand-order sum, which has the closed form
$$
\sum_{i}(a_i + 1) \;=\; \Omega_{\max}(\mathrm{Sp}_n) + m \;=\; T_n + n \;=\; \frac{n(n+3)}{2} \;=\; \frac{(n+1)(n+2)}{2} - 1,
$$
against the flat node count $\tau(\mathrm{Sp}_n) = (n+1)!$. The product-vs-sum ratio is therefore
$$
\frac{\tau(\mathrm{Sp}_n)}{\sum_i (a_i+1)} \;=\; \frac{(n+1)!}{n(n+3)/2},
$$
super-exponential in $n$: $\approx 6.7\times10^4$ at $\mathrm{Sp}_9$, $\approx 6.1\times10^5$ at $\mathrm{Sp}_{10}$, $\approx 6.9\times10^7$ at $\mathrm{Sp}_{12}$. The superprimorial family is the canonical extremal witness of the §10.8(a) cost map precisely because it minimizes symmetry-strand growth (one new prime per rank) against maximal node growth — the regime where structured per-strand computation wins most decisively, and where the margin *increases* with lattice size rather than saturating. The strand-order sum is the Rmk 9.7k local-comb cardinality (Part II / II.18) offset by $(m-1)$: $\sum_i(a_i+1) = |\mathrm{comb}(d)| + (m-1) = (1 + \Omega_{\max}) + (m-1)$.

*Companion verification.* The companion script `superprimorial_scale.py` checks the four closed forms ($\tau = (n+1)!$, $m = n$, $\Sigma = (n+1)(n+2)/2 - 1$, ratio $= \tau/\Sigma$) two ways — by direct sympy divisor enumeration and by closed form, with agreement required. Constructive enumeration runs to $n = 7$ ($\mathrm{Sp}_7$, 40,320 nodes; all checks PASS); $n = 8$–$12$ are closed-form, sound because $\tau(\mathrm{Sp}_n) = (n+1)!$ is unconditional (Rmk 9.7l). The run reproduces the cited ratios exactly: 67,200 ($\mathrm{Sp}_9$), 614,105 ($\mathrm{Sp}_{10}$), 69,189,120 ($\mathrm{Sp}_{12}$). The large superprimorials are not enumerated and need not be — the closed forms are proven identities, not empirical estimates.

*Rendering consequence (tooling — companion-documentation scope).* At superprimorial scale, any practical full-scene readout must exploit the structural decomposition; flat one-primitive-per-node rendering is a tooling anti-pattern, not the kernel-faithful route. The structural means are already kernel objects — the viewer's $\Omega$-cross-section and fibered-mode readouts are the Rmk 9.7d three structural readouts. Hardware-specific rendering and heavy-compute strategy (instanced rendering, mode-wise operator contraction, CPU-vs-GPU placement) belong to the Serena / viewer companion documentation and to VI.7, not to the kernel, per the project documentation policy. This is an implementation constraint, not a kernel claim: whether a given readout "runs" depends on viewer mode, hardware, memory budget, and which operation is attempted.

> *The atlas classification is already a cost map: an atom's signature shape fixes its symmetry-speedup class, profile-equivalent atoms transport profile-register results for free, and that transport stops exactly at the edge of the profile register — where any finer register, prime-rank first, begins.*

*Concrete instances.* Profile class $(1,1,1)$: $\mathrm{Div}(30)$, $\mathrm{Div}(1001)$, $\mathrm{Div}(2 \cdot 3 \cdot 7)$, … — all $\cong B_3$, all $S_3$, all $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}} = 0$. Profile class $(4,2,1)$: $\mathrm{Div}(720) = 2^4 \cdot 3^2 \cdot 5$, $\mathrm{Div}(2^4 \cdot 3^2 \cdot 7)$, $\mathrm{Div}(3^4 \cdot 2^2 \cdot 5)$, … — all lattice-rigid, all carrying the canonical instrument's strained cost.

## VI.7 Mathematical visualization

**Source:** Companion-artifact summaries (tooling).  
**v3.0 location:** Part VI / VI.7.  
**Status:** tooling.

The viewer / trainer ecosystem comprises companion artifacts that render Parts I–V structures interactively: Universal Lattice Trainer (VI.8), Newton Signature panel (VI.9), Helix and Garcia viewers (VI.10), Collatz-on-lattice tooling (VI.11), Lattice Path Racer (VI.14). These are companion artifacts, not kernel theorems; cross-references support the kernel content rather than extending it.

The FVLT Fourier viewer (`fvlt_fourier__14_.html`) implements Algorithm Note ALG-1's single-phasor divisor gate at reference gearbox reduction. The viewer surfaces a 6-parameter operating family $(k, \psi_3, s, \alpha, t_1, t_2)$ documented in companion `fvlt_fourier_instrument_spec_v0.md`; under simultaneous reduction $\psi_3 = 1$, $t_1 = t_2 = 1$, $s \in \mathbb{Z}$, the algorithm constructs the factorial-shell divisor lattice $\mathrm{Div}(k!)$.

The cyclotomic-order panel (Panel B) of the FVLT Fourier viewer implements Algorithm Note ALG-2: at fixed $s$, plotting $m(N, s)$ vs $N$ resolves the gcd stratification as a set of linear diagonal branches indexed by $\mathrm{Div}(s)$. The viewer supports a $m_0$ / $m_{\mathrm{plot}}$ / $m_1$ display toggle to distinguish the three field-theoretic invariants; see `fvlt_fourier_viewer_patch_plan_alg2.md` for implementation details.

Continuous-$N$ winding between integer divisor nodes — the geometric content of Algorithm Note ALG-3 — is computable in the viewer by integrating the angular rate $s\pi/N^2$ between integer endpoints. Visualization options include unwrapped-angle trace, winding-number annotation between adjacent divisor pairs, and atomic-ratio highlighting (where the recovered $r$ is a prime factor of $s$).

The architectural pattern under which these viewers operate — classical arithmetic backend supplying factorizations, cached lattice assembly via CRT component reuse, instruments as subscribers to a shared compute state, ALG-3 as continuous transition readout layer — is specified in Architecture Note ANL-2 (Free Navigation Extension); see `anl_2_public_entry.md`. Per ANL-2's category boundary, the viewers and the framework as a whole do not autonomously factor integers; the classical backend supplies the facts, the lattice layer makes those facts navigable, the instruments supply the readout.

## VI.10 Helix and Garcia viewers

**Source:** helix_lattice.html + Garcia mode supercharacter viewer (companion artifacts, April 2026).  
**v3.0 location:** Part VI / VI.10.  
**Status:** tooling.

`helix_lattice.html` overview: Helix and Lattice modes; apex defect $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}$ readout (Part II / II.15); presets including $\mathrm{Div}(900)$, $\mathrm{Div}(720720)$. Renders the multi-prime polygonal embedding (Definition 9.7) and the Hasse–helix correspondence (Lemma 9.7a).

**Exponent register as strand geometry (v3.1.1 tooling note).** The helix viewer renders the exponent register as strand geometry: a prime-direction strand has order $n_i = a_i + 1$, radius $1/n_i$, angular step $2\pi/n_i$, and $a_i$ cover-edges; the prime label itself enters only through phase/color. The exponent register and the prime register (Rmk 9.7g) are thus rendered as two orthogonal visual channels — geometry and phase. Consequently the local comb (the $m$ strands through any selected divisor, each a complete prime-$i$ fiber) is a visual readout of the exponent profile $(a_1, \ldots, a_m)$: counting cover-edges per strand recovers the profile. This is the viewer-side geometric analogue of the divisor-index $\to$ exponent-profile $\to$ path-product-spectrum route established in the C3d verdict (`c3d_verdict.md`). Tooling-level observation, code-verified against `helixBead` / `divisorPosition`; no theorem content affected.

**Garcia mode supercharacter viewer (April 2026).** CRT component decomposition panel, subgroup catalog, image energy distribution panel. Key finding (preserved): composite Garcia images need not inherit visible geometry from matching components — global form arises from CRT interaction. Garcia mode is an *orthogonal structural probe of FVLT shell moduli*. Basis shell ($720$) is subgroup-sparse ($\lambda = 12$); complement shell ($1001$, $\lambda = 60$) is Kloosterman-rich. Garcia mode operates on the algebraic side of Rmk 9.7h's CRT fibered presentation (Part II / II.16); no Garcia-specific kernel theorems assumed.

## VI.12 Serena and sovereign-AI instrumentation

**Source:** Serena scope (research-track; March–April 2026 milestones).

**Status:** research-track / application.

The Serena sovereign-AI stack instruments Parts I–V at infrastructure scale. Three-lobe architecture (Nucleus / Membrane / Scout) separates reasoning, orchestration, and perception across distinct compute roles; specific hardware, model, database, and networking choices live in Serena's companion documentation rather than in the kernel.

Four-tier staged congruence ladder (cheap → screen → FVLT → full) implements Parts I–V's diagnostic-priority structure as an epistemic control plane. Permanent manual gate on interpretive writes. StepLog with metrics_computed.

Serena v3.1.1 (March 2026) achieves Phase 1 (congruence) + Phase 2 (stack doc) + orchestrator rewrite. Genesis layer (April 2026): orbit decomposition of $(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^\times$ derives Ramanujan sums as supercharacters — the substrate self-constructs (Part II / II.2 + II.8). Genesis pipeline (`genesis_pipeline.html`) implements Steps 0–5 of the FVLT pipeline (group → action → orbits → characters → supercharacter → profile).

## VI.13 Position-awareness as anti-hallucination discipline

**Source:** Note 9.7m application-half (v2.7.14).  
**v3.0 location:** Part VI / VI.13.  
**Status:** interpretive.

**Note 9.7m application half (preserved).** A computational or interpretive agent — including an LLM operating on FVLT-substrate data — situated at a divisor $d \in \mathrm{Div}(N)$ can verify its claimed position against the three locally-checkable invariants of Part II / II.18: cardinality $|\mathrm{comb}(d)| = 1 + \Omega_{\max}$, polygon-order multiset $\{a_i + 1\}_{i=1}^{m}$, strand-position vector $(e_i(d))_{i=1}^{m}$.

A claim of position at divisor $d$ that contradicts the local-comb cardinality has demonstrably misidentified its position. *"Hallucination is structurally constrained by inconsistency with locally-checkable invariants."*

**Refusal of overclaim (preserved).** Position-awareness does *not eliminate hallucination*; it is one anti-hallucination property among several. The structural availability of the local invariants supports proactive position-verification by AI agents operating on FVLT-substrate data. The invariants supply *what to check*; whether the agent checks them is a system-design question, not a kernel-theorem question.

**Superprimorial case (preserved).** For $\mathrm{Sp}_n$ (Part III / III.7 Rmk 9.7l), the polygon signature $\{2, 3, \ldots, n+1\}$ is a high-information fingerprint with no gaps — the framework's canonical instances for position-awareness audits. $\mathrm{Sp}_6$ in particular ($\tau = 5040$, $\Omega_{\max} = 21$, multiset $\{2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7\}$) supplies a rich auditable structure.

## VI.14 Lattice racer and training tasks

**Source:** §F companion-spine (Phase 1.a complete); routing only.  
**v3.0 location:** Part VI / VI.14.  
**Status:** routed / definitional / applications.

Five task families for training AI agents on FVLT-substrate operation: navigation (find specified divisor under given constraints), reconstruction (rebuild lattice from partial information), bridge finding (identify proper bridges in $\mathrm{Div}(720720)$), position-awareness (verify claimed position against local invariants per VI.13), classification (place an atom in the Part III taxonomy).

Curriculum gradient: lattice fully drawn → progressively occluded → only $N$ given. Three implementation tiers: L1 perceptual fluency / L2 partial-occlusion reconstruction / L3a signed navigation winding-tolerant / L3b winding-sensitive (blocked by §E.4, Part IV / IV.13).

**§F companion-spine routing note (terse, per Robin's File 1 verdict 2).** §F companion-spine body carries the formal task specification: Def F.1 (lattice racer task) → inputs (lattice $N$, query type, occlusion level) → move budget (step / time bound) → scoring (correctness + efficiency) → curriculum (drawing reduction schedule) → three tiers (L1 / L2 / L3a / L3b). Status: definitional / applications; not theorem-bearing in v3.0. Phase 1.a green; five flags applied during audit. Full §F body remains routed; body-inclusion is post-v3.0.

# Part VII — Status, Scope, Open Problems, Notation, Bibliography

Part VII is the integrity contract. Every claim in Parts I–VI is auditable against Part VII's status partition, scope discipline, and open-problem index. The annotation vocabulary is frozen; the status partition is non-annotative; the open problems are explicit.

## VII.1 Annotation and status vocabulary

**Source:** Front-matter vocabulary block (v2.2 frozen); §1.3 (v2.7.14); v2.7.x lived-practice qualifier ledger.  
**v3.0 location:** Part VII / VII.1.  
**Status:** definitional (governance).

The frozen annotation vocabulary (v2.2, preserved verbatim from front-matter comment block):

| Field | Values |
|---|---|
| `register` | proved \| definitional \| remark \| interpretive \| empirical \| conditional \| example |
| `status` | unconditional \| conditional \| empirical \| definitional \| interpretive \| open |
| `provenance` | original \| classical \| adapted |
| `depends` | comma-separated item numbers (e.g., 3.14, 4.9) \| none |
| `id format` | `PREFIX-NUM-SLUG` where `PREFIX ∈ {THM, DEF, COR, RMK, OBS, NOTE, EX}`, `SLUG ≤ 40 chars uppercase` |

**§1.3 Proof Status Convention (preserved verbatim):**

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Unconditional | Proved from axioms; no unproven conjectures assumed |
| Conditional | Assumes Goldbach's conjecture (verified for $n \leq 4 \times 10^{18}$) |
| Empirical | Computationally verified; proof not yet established |

To these are added — preserved across the v2.7.x series — the working labels *definitional*, *interpretive*, and *open*. The partition is the integrity contract.

**v2.7.x lived-practice qualifiers (cleared by audit, preserved as compound forms only).** `structural` and `structural scope` may appear as block-level qualifiers but never replace one of the core six status labels. Disallowed as standalone status labels: `instrumental`, `architectural`, `diagnostic`, `tooling`, `research-track` — these may appear in prose only.

Compound forms `; depends on X` and `; X-qualifier` follow Chris audit verdict: convention varies by block, but the core status label is always one of the six.

Cross-referenced by ID throughout Parts I–VI.

**ALG-1 — Single-Phasor Divisor Gate for Hasse Atom Construction**

**Statement:** At integer spin $s \in \mathbb{N}_{>0}$ and integer operating shell $N \geq 1$, the single-phasor imaginary part vanishes if and only if $N$ divides $s$:
$$\Im(z_1(N, s)) = 0 \iff N \mid s.$$

**Status:** Cleared (V1/V2 PASS recorded May 13, 2026; consolidated in `phasor_stack_final_audit_ingestion_packet.md` May 23, 2026).

**Dependencies:** Euler Bridge decomposition (`fvlt_fourier_instrument_spec_v0.md` §5); reference $t_2=1$ six-parameter family specialization (`fvlt_fourier_instrument_spec_v0.md` §1, §4).

**Distinction:** The Q-residual $\Im(z_1 + z_2)$ is a structural symmetry of the antipodal gearbox at integer spin (Lemma II.23.2) and vanishes for all integer $s$; it is **not** the divisor detector. The single-phasor imaginary part is.

**Cross-reference:** ALG-1's divisor gate is the divisor-contact register of Rmk II.23.2c; it is disjoint in mechanism from Q-residual collapse (Cor II.23.2.1, exact locus Cor II.23.2.2).

**ALG-2 — Cyclotomic Stratification of the Single-Phasor Curve at Integer Spin**

**Statement:** At integer spin $s$ and integer $N$, the normalized single-phasor $z_1/N^\alpha$ is a primitive root of unity whose cyclotomic order is determined by $d = \gcd(s, N)$, $p = s/d$, $q = N/d$. Three field-theoretic invariants are distinguished:
$$m_0(N, s) = \frac{2q}{\gcd(p, 2q)}, \quad m_{\mathrm{plot}}(N, s) = \frac{2N}{\gcd((N-1)s, 2N)}, \quad m_1(N, s) = q.$$
The map $N \mapsto (d, p, q)$ stratifies $\mathbb{N}_{>0}$ into $\gcd$-classes indexed by divisors of $s$, with each stratum $S_d$ having size $\varphi(s/d)$ in the fundamental window $\{1, \ldots, s\}$ and size $k \cdot \varphi(s/d)$ in $\{1, \ldots, ks\}$.

**Status:** Cleared (V1/V2 PASS recorded May 23, 2026 via `phasor_stack_final_audit_ingestion_packet.md` after $k \cdot \varphi(s/d)$ stratum-size correction).

**Dependencies:** Euler Bridge decomposition (`fvlt_fourier_instrument_spec_v0.md` §5); ALG-1 (contact stratum recovery as $m_1 = 1$ case).

**Distinction:** $m_0$, $m_{\mathrm{plot}}$, $m_1$ are three distinct field-theoretic invariants with documented interconversions and parity relationships. Plots that collapse divisors to "$m = 1$" use the contact-normalized convention ($m_1$) and must label as such. $m_0 = m_{\mathrm{plot}}$ at even $s$; may differ by parity factor at odd $s$.

**ALG-3 — Continuous-Phasor Winding Recovery of Divisor Lattice Ratios**

**Statement:** For positive integer spin $s$, the canonical phasor angle $\theta(N) = (N-1)s\pi/N = s\pi - s\pi/N$ extends continuously in $N \in \mathbb{R}_{>0}$ as the **unwrapped** angle (not the principal-branch argument). For divisibility chain $N_1 \mid N_2$ with both inside $\mathrm{Div}(s)$, the angular winding is
$$\Delta\theta(N_1, N_2; s) = \int_{N_1}^{N_2} \frac{s\pi}{N^2}\, dN = s\pi\left(\frac{1}{N_1} - \frac{1}{N_2}\right) = \pi \ell (r - 1),$$
with $r = N_2/N_1$, $\ell = s/N_2$. The ratio $r$ is recoverable as $r = (\Delta\theta/\pi)/\ell + 1$.

**Status:** Cleared (V1/V2 PASS recorded May 23, 2026 via `phasor_stack_final_audit_ingestion_packet.md`).

**Dependencies:** Unwrapped-angle convention (instrument spec §13); ALG-1 (divisor vertex identification as endpoint anchoring).

**Distinction:** ALG-3 recovers divisor-lattice **ratios** via continuous winding (geometric content). Hasse adjacency in $\mathrm{Div}(s)$ requires the additional **combinatorial atomicity test** that $r$ is prime — equivalent to $r$ being atomic in $\mathrm{Div}(s)$. Geometry recovers ratios; lattice combinatorics decides which ratios are atomic.

**ALG-5 — Paired-Residual Branch Classifier and Dyadic Valuation Ladder**

**Statement:** Work in the nondegenerate paired Vernier gearbox of Cor II.23.2.2: $z_1 = r\,e^{i(n_3 t_1 - t_2)s\pi/n_3}$, $z_2 = r\,e^{i(n_3 t_1 + t_2)s\pi/n_3}$, with $n_3 > 0$, real $r \neq 0$, $t_2 \neq 0$; $A = t_1 s\pi$, $B = t_2 s\pi/n_3$. Three readout steps:

*(S1) Branch classifier.* Every Q-zero ($\operatorname{Im}(z_1+z_2)=0$) carries at least one of two labels, and the label set is exhaustive by Cor II.23.2.2(iv):
$$\textsf{ALIGN}: t_1 s \in \mathbb{Z} \quad (e^{2iA}=1,\ \text{conjugacy}), \qquad \textsf{SPREAD}: \tfrac{t_2 s}{n_3} \in \mathbb{Z}+\tfrac12 \quad (e^{2iB}=-1,\ \text{cancellation without pairing}),$$
with $\textsf{ABSORBED}$ = both. No Q-zero is unlabeled; no non-zero point is labeled. This is Rmk II.23.2c's guard — *off-lattice Q-zeros must not be read as conjugate-pair witnesses* — made executable as a decision procedure over the continuous Q-residual sweep.

*(S2) Spacing readout.* The $\textsf{SPREAD}$ zeros form the arithmetic progression $s = n_3(2k+1)/(2t_2)$, $k \in \mathbb{Z}$, with spacing exactly $n_3/t_2$; a continuous-$s$ sweep of the paired residual recovers $n_3$ at known $t_2$ from dip spacing alone. The continuum enters as an integration variable along the residual curve, not as an arithmetic object (ALG-3 posture).

*(S3) Dyadic valuation ladder.* At $t_1 = 1$, step the spread gear through $t_2 = 2^j$, $j = 0, 1, 2, \ldots$ Absorption of the $\textsf{SPREAD}$ family into the alignment lattice is **all-or-nothing at each rung**: the point $s = n_3(2k+1)/2^{j+1}$ is an integer iff $j+1 \leq v_2(n_3)$, and since $v_2(n_3(2k+1)) = v_2(n_3)$ for every $k$ (odd cofactor), the condition is $k$-independent. Therefore
$$v_2(n_3) \;=\; \min\{\, j \geq 0 : \textsf{SPREAD} \text{ zeros are off-lattice at } t_2 = 2^j \,\}.$$
The paired residual reads the 2-adic valuation of $n_3$ — one exponent coordinate of the divisor-lattice profile, one factor of the Cartesian decomposition (Thm K.2) — from the cancellation spectrum, with no division performed. Cor II.23.2.2(vi) (parity absorption) is the $j = 0$ rung.

**Scope:** The pair's spread branch is $\zeta_2$ on the doubled spread phase (Cor II.23.2.2(iv)); the two-tooth gearbox is intrinsically a **dyadic instrument**. There is no odd-prime analogue within the resident pair. The integer divisor scan (Rmk II.5a, ALG-1) never meets the $\textsf{SPREAD}$ progression; ALG-5 operates on the continuous residual readout.

**Status:** Cleared (Opus cold audit PASS 2026-07-04; companion `verify_alg5.py` ALL PASS, byte-reproduced at seed 30030 with 27,872 detected Q-zeros). Unconditional-tier claims (S1)–(S3), each an elementary consequence of Cor II.23.2.2 plus the $k$-independence identity $v_2(n_3(2k+1)) = v_2(n_3)$. Verification record: S1 over 40,000 randomized samples (27,872 detected Q-zeros, unlabeled 0, false labels 0); S2 over 2,000 samples (spacing exact, zeros genuine); S3 exhaustive over the full $n_1 = 6$ shell $n_3 \in 1..721$ at $j \in 0..11$ (all-or-nothing at every rung; ladder $= v_2(n_3)$ everywhere) plus 200 random $n_3 \leq 10^9$. Bibliography-tier readout instrument (Algorithm Note) parallel to ALG-1/2/3; the II.23.2.2 prerequisite is satisfied as of the v3.2.3 commit.

**Dependencies:** II.23.2.2 (all three steps); II.23.2, II.23.2.1 through it. Cross-references: II.23.2c (S1 is its guard made executable); II.5a, ALG-1 (scan-register disjointness); K.2 (exponent coordinate read by S3); II.23.8 (measurement-side mirror).

**Distinction:** ALG-1 reads set membership (single-phasor divisor gate); ALG-2 reads gcd strata; ALG-3 reads lattice ratios via continuous winding. ALG-5 is the **first pair-side readout**: it reads from $\operatorname{Im}(z_1+z_2)$, not from a single phasor or a winding, and it returns a **$p$-adic exponent coordinate** ($p = 2$) rather than a set, a stratum, or a ratio. Mirror relation: II.23.8 localizes $\zeta_q^k$ coherence breaks on the measurement side; ALG-5 classifies $\zeta_2$ cancellations on the generative/phasor side. Firewalls (standing): $v_2$ is classically trivial — the content is that the exponent coordinate is **native to the phasor register**, not that it is newly computable; no factoring advantage claimed; no quantum advantage claimed (Remark II.22a register discipline). **Reserved-designation separation:** ALG-5 is distinct from the reserved ALG-4 / RES-3 question — ALG-4 names the open bridge-architecture readout problem (recovering $\mathrm{Br}_3^{\mathrm{pos}}$ pairings from the phasor stack); ALG-5 reads $p$-adic exponent coordinates and makes no bridge claim. The two are different questions on different structures; neither subsumes the other.

**ANL-2 (Free Navigation Extension, v3.1.13).** The FVLT phasor stack (ALG-1, ALG-2, ALG-3) composes with a classical arithmetic backend and a shared compute layer to form a distributed coordinate-readout architecture in four layers: classical arithmetic backend → cached lattice assembly (CRT component reuse + global axis recomposition, derivable from Thm K.2) → shared compute layer (subscriber pattern) → ALG-3 continuous transition readout. Category boundary: classical algorithms supply the facts; the lattice makes them navigable; instruments supply the readout. FVLT does not autonomously factor integers. Empirically grounded by the C3 experiment (May 24, 2026; `c3_verdict.md`): combinatorial-geometric invariants DIRECT, spectral invariants DOWNSTREAM-DERIVABLE, bridge architecture SEPARATE LAYER. See `anl_2_public_entry.md` for the architecture note.

## VII.3 Scope discipline

**Source:** §1.1, §1.2, Rmk 1.1 (v2.7.14); Lucretian Ratioque-eight-principles framing (v2.7.x).  
**v3.0 location:** Part VII / VII.3.  
**Status:** governance / interpretive.

### §1.1 Foundational Principle (preserved verbatim)

**The structure is the algorithm.**

**FVLT is a resolution theory indexed by factorial depth.** The transform is the measurement apparatus. The framework is about what distinctions are available — what can be resolved — at a given structural depth. Results are terminal rather than asymptotic because the limits are architectural, not computational.

Ratioque operates strictly within the permutation group $S_k$, not on arbitrary integers. All computational speedups derive from this choice of basis. The geometry of the factorial lattice eliminates redundant computation (GCD checks, resonance verification) by defining them as structural impossibilities rather than runtime queries.

**Lucretian governing principle.** *Naturae species ratioque* — the look and logic of nature. The framework's methodology is descriptive, not prescriptive. It shows what IS and what MEANS; it does not prescribe what to do.

**Ratioque eight principles (carried throughout the framework):**

(1) Perception over prediction. (2) Description over prescription. (3) Questions over answers. (4) Transparency over authority. (5) Accessibility over sophistication. (6) Civic purpose over private advantage. (7) Memory over forgetting. (8) Humility over certainty — clinamen is the foundation.

The framework shows what IS and MEANS, not what to do. It can say "this harms X," not "therefore do Y." It generates inquiry, not conclusions. Summary first, complexity on demand.

### §1.2 Scope and Limitations (preserved verbatim)

To prevent misinterpretation:

| Claim to Avoid | Reality |
|---|---|
| "Proof of Goldbach" | We prove *purity* properties conditioned on factorial moduli (Theorem 6.2, Part III / III.11). Coverage (Theorem 6.3) assumes Goldbach. |
| "$O(1)$ for everything" | $O(1)$ coprimality applies specifically to prime vs. factorial modulus with $k$ fixed. |
| "Competes with FFT" | FFT and FVLT operate on **different symmetries** — they are complementary, not competing tools. |
| "Numerology" | All claims are algebraically proven or explicitly marked as conditional/empirical. |

<!-- id: RMK-1.1-FFT-VS-FVLT | register: remark | status: unconditional | provenance: original | depends: none -->
**Remark 1.1 (FFT vs FVLT, preserved).** FFT decomposes signals into sinusoidal basis functions $e^{2\pi i kn/N}$ with continuous rotational symmetry; FVLT decomposes into Ramanujan sums $c_q(n)$ with discrete multiplicative symmetry. FFT asks: "What sinusoidal frequencies are present?" FVLT asks: "What divisibility resonances are present?" Complementary for signals where discrete modular patterns dominate over smooth periodic ones.

## VII.4 Closure-lattice portability

**Source:** Note 9.7n (v2.7.13/14).  
**v3.0 location:** Part VII / VII.4.  
**Status:** structural scope.

<!-- id: NOTE-9.7n-CLOSURE-LATTICE-PORTABILITY | register: note | status: structural | provenance: original | depends: RMK-1.5b-CLOSURE-SUBSTRATE, 9.7, 9.7b, 9.7c, 9.7f -->
**Note 9.7n (Closure-Lattice Portability is Conditional, v2.7.13, preserved verbatim).** The §9.7 architecture is specifically the *geometric realization of the arithmetic divisor lattice*. Its theorems — Hasse–helix correspondence (Lem 9.7a, Part II / II.14), cancellation law (Lem 9.7c, Part II / II.15), strain non-additivity under join (Rmk 9.7f, Part V / V.4) — are unconditional inside that arithmetic substrate but conditional outside it.

A closure-lattice abstraction — replacing $\mathrm{Div}(N)$ with a concept lattice $\Lambda_{\mathrm{desc}}(O, \Sigma, I)$ via Formal Concept Analysis (Galois closure between extents and intents, cf. Rmk 1.5b / Part II / II.19) — gives an architectural template, *not an automatic theorem schema*. Each component must be re-earned from the extracted substrate.

| Component | Arithmetic §9.7 | Closure-lattice abstraction |
|---|---|---|
| closure object | $\mathrm{Div}(N)$ | $\Lambda_{\mathrm{desc}}(O, \Sigma, I)$ via FCA Galois closure |
| rank | $\Omega(d) = \sum e_i$, canonical | must be declared (specificity depth, closure-size depth, etc.) |
| covers | multiply by one prime | one-step semantic refinement / closure cover |
| phase / bank assignment | canonical prime ordering, $\varphi_i = 2\pi i/m$ | declared semantic axes; orthogonality not free |
| cancellation law | unconditional (character orthogonality on $\mathbb{Z}/m$) | conditional on chosen phase/bank orthogonality |
| strain | unconditional once embedding is fixed | diagnostic, not theorem-level until tested |
| join | strain non-additivity proven (Rmk 9.7f) | architectural caution: ontology joins must recompute strain globally |

**Substrate extraction (four sequential conditions).** §9.7 transfers to an ontology lattice only after:

1. A typed property graph is closed into a concept lattice via FCA.
2. The lattice is ranked by an explicit $\Omega_{\mathrm{desc}}$.
3. Semantic axes are declared and assigned to banks.
4. Phase orthogonality is computed on the chosen bank assignment, or shown to fail.

Substrate extraction is upstream of every §9.7 mechanism; skipping it produces visualization without warrant.

**Join warning.** *Ontology joins must recompute strain globally.* Merged lattices may introduce phase-redistribution strain through shared axes — *person, date, approval, obligation, authority* — that no fragment exhibits alone. That strain is the semantic equivalent of the $720720$ phase-redistribution result.

**L1c containment.** L1c (banked attention over ontology lattices, parallel to L1a CVA over $\mathrm{Div}(720)$) is *active research track, not kernel theorem*. §9.7 / v2.7.13 holds arithmetic geometry locked; this note marks the scope boundary. Smallest real L1c test: FCA on Serena's property graph, helix-lattice viewer aimed at $\Lambda_{\mathrm{desc}}$. *Raw graph first, closure lattice second, geometry third.* Pass/fail criterion: if the crystal's closures and strains match editorial sense, L1c has a working substrate; if not, the failure localizes to substrate extraction. Falsifiable without putting kernel theorems at risk.

> *The multi-prime embedding provides an architectural template, not an automatic theorem schema: outside $\mathrm{Div}(N)$, closure, rank, phase, bank, and cancellation must be re-earned from the extracted substrate.*

## VII.5 Limit structures

**Source:** §14.1, §14.2, §14.2a, §14.3 (v2.7.14); Thm 14.2, Def 14.3, Thm 14.4, Rmks 14.4a, 14.4b.  
**v3.0 location:** Part VII / VII.5.  
**Status:** per §14 block-level.

### §14.1 The Prime Limit

For $n = p$ prime, $K(p) = \{1, 2, \ldots, p - 1\}$ and the sumset $U + U$ covers almost all of $\mathbb{Z}/p$ (Cauchy–Davenport).

<!-- id: THM-14.2-WITT-RECOVERY | register: research-proposal | status: open | provenance: original | depends: none -->
**Open Dependency 14.2 (Witt Recovery, not active).**
$$
\lim_{p \to \infty} \mathrm{witt}_p^{\mathrm{cop}} / I_p \;\cong\; \mathrm{witt}.
$$
This legacy recovery claim depends on quotient data not established in v3.2.0 and is not active theorem authority.

### §14.2 The Factorial Inverse Limit

<!-- id: DEF-14.3-PRO-HEISENBERG | register: proposed-construction | status: research-proposal | provenance: original | depends: 12.3, 12.4 -->
**Definition 14.3 (Pro-Heisenberg Algebra, proposed/historical).**
$$
\hat{\mathfrak{h}}_! \;=\; \varprojlim_m \left(\mathrm{witt}_{m!}^{\mathrm{cop}} / I_{m!}\right).
$$
This object depends on the quarantined quotient branch and is not active in v3.2.0.

<!-- id: THM-14.4-PROFINITE-INDEXING | register: group-fact | status: active-arithmetic-fact | provenance: adapted | depends: none -->
**Arithmetic/Group Fact 14.4 (Factorial-unit inverse limit, active).**
$$
\varprojlim_m (\mathbb{Z}/m!\mathbb{Z})^\times \;\cong\; \widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^\times.
$$
No Heisenberg, Fock, or quotient consequence is attached to this active fact.

### §14.2a Structural coherence of the inverse limit

The group-level inverse system of units inherits structural coherence from the factorial filtration: transition maps between unit groups are compatible, and the projective limit exists. The legacy algebraic inverse limit remains quarantined with Def 14.3.

<!-- id: RMK-14.4a-CARDINALITY | register: group-fact | status: active-arithmetic-fact | provenance: original | depends: 14.4 -->
**Remark 14.4a (Cardinality of the Unit Limit, active arithmetic/group fact).** Each finite group $(\mathbb{Z}/m!\mathbb{Z})^\times$ has $\varphi(m!)$ elements. The union of finite-stage labels is countable ($\aleph_0$). The inverse limit $\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^\times$ has cardinality $2^{\aleph_0}$ (the continuum). No Fock-space or Heisenberg conclusion follows from this cardinality statement.

<!-- id: RMK-14.4b-INTERNAL-CH | register: remark | status: interpretive | provenance: original | depends: 14.4a -->
**Remark 14.4b (Internal Cardinality Observation, interpretive).** Within the factorial tower construction, the only cardinalities that appear are: finite, countable ($\aleph_0$), continuum ($2^{\aleph_0}$). No intermediate cardinality appears inside this declared construction. This does not constitute a proof of CH in full ZFC generality — proving CH would require showing no set of any kind has intermediate cardinality, which is independent of ZFC by Gödel/Cohen.

### §14.3 The Adelic Perspective

The profinite indexing $\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^\times \cong \prod_p \mathbb{Z}_p^\times$ supplies an adelic perspective: each prime-power local sector contributes a $\mathbb{Z}_p^\times$ component to the limit, and the full limit is the restricted product.

## VII.6 Structural recognition and autonomy

**Source:** §14.2b, §14.2c, §14.2d (v2.7.14); Rmk 14.4c (v2.7.14).  
**v3.0 location:** Part VII / VII.6.  
**Status:** per §14 block-level / interpretive.

### §14.2b Autonomy and coherent extension in the factorial tower

At each level of the factorial tower, the unit group $(\mathbb{Z}/k!\mathbb{Z})^\times$ is determined by $k!$. Coherent extension up the tower preserves the group-level inverse-system data. The old algebra-autonomy reading is quarantined with Def 14.3.

### §14.2c The sumset obstruction as anti-autonomy constraint

<!-- id: RMK-14.4c-PARITY-LOCK | register: arithmetic-fact | status: active-arithmetic-fact | provenance: original | depends: none -->
**Remark 14.4c (Parity Lock Arithmetic Fact, active).** For $k \geq 2$, $k!$ is even, so units modulo $k!$ are odd and
$$
(K(k!) + K(k!)) \cap K(k!) = \emptyset.
$$

For all $k \geq 2$, $2 \mid k!$. Therefore the coprime set $K(k!)$ consists entirely of odd residues. The sum of two odd residues is even, hence not a unit modulo $k!$. This is the full active content of the parity-lock statement in v3.2.0.

| Path | Moduli | Parity of $N$ | Coprime parity | Sumset status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Factorial: $k!$ | $2, 6, 24, 120, 720, \ldots$ | Even ($k \geq 2$) | All odd | Obstruction holds |
| Prime: $p$ | $2, 3, 5, 7, 11, 13, \ldots$ | Odd ($p > 2$) | Mixed | Obstruction fails |

The bifurcation is at $N = 2$: the smallest factorial $2! = 2$ and the smallest prime $p = 2$ are the same modulus. At this single point the paths overlap. For all subsequent levels they diverge, *and the divergence is permanent because the factorial tower can never return to odd moduli*.

**Structural boundary.** The arithmetic parity observation for even moduli remains available. Quotient, CCR, Fock, pro-Heisenberg, and Witt-recovery consequences require future definitions and proofs and are not active in v3.2.0.

Cross-references live: Rmk II.3a, Rmk II.3b, Note 9.7n, THM-14.4, Rmk 14.4a, Rmk 14.4c. ### Note 14.5 — P-adic Completion of the Atlas

The p-adic extension of the atlas is not an added decorative layer. It is the completion of structures already present in the finite atoms.

The finite divisor atom

\[
N = \prod_i p_i^{a_i}
\]

carries, at each prime axis, a capped valuation coordinate

\[
e_i \in \{0,1,\ldots,a_i\}.
\]

The signature register of Rmk II.3a is therefore already a finite p-adic valuation window:

\[
\sigma_N(n)=\bigl(\min(v_{p_i}(n),a_i)\bigr)_i.
\]

The atlas did not need p-adics appended to it. Each finite divisor lattice is a capped window onto the valuation-vector substrate.

---

### 1. Finite register split: valuation class × unit part

For every residue class \(n \in \mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z\), let

\[
d = \gcd(n,N).
\]

Then \(d\mid N\), and the quotient residue

\[
u = n/d
\]

is a unit modulo \(N/d\):

\[
u \in (\mathbb Z/(N/d)\mathbb Z)^\times.
\]

Equivalently, the residue system decomposes as a disjoint union

\[
\mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z
\cong
\coprod_{d\,\mid\,N}
 d\cdot (\mathbb Z/(N/d)\mathbb Z)^\times.
\]

The counting identity is

\[
\sum_{d\mid N}\varphi(N/d)=N.
\]

This is the finite shadow of the multiplicative p-adic decomposition

\[
\mathbb Q_p^\times \cong p^{\mathbb Z}\times \mathbb Z_p^\times.
\]

The **position/Hasse register** reads the valuation factor: divisor label, capped exponent vector, Hasse position. The **harmonic register** reads the unit factor: conductor subspaces, Ramanujan channels, gcd-orbits, and exact phase data.

**Status.** The finite decomposition and counting identity are exact. The identification of the two-register discipline as the finite completion-pattern of \(p^{\mathbb Z}\times \mathbb Z_p^\times\) is interpretive / proposed.

---

### 2. Three completions

#### 2.1 Depth completion: prime strands

Each prime-power strand

\[
0\to 1\to \cdots \to a_i
\]

is a capped valuation chain. Letting the cap grow gives the infinite valuation ray

\[
0\to1\to2\to\cdots.
\]

This order ray is not itself \(\mathbb Z_p\). The ring \(\mathbb Z_p\) enters through coherent residue data across the inverse system

\[
\mathbb Z/p\mathbb Z \leftarrow \mathbb Z/p^2\mathbb Z \leftarrow \mathbb Z/p^3\mathbb Z \leftarrow \cdots.
\]

**Guard.** The order-completion of a valuation chain and the ring inverse limit \(\mathbb Z_p\) are different objects. Conflating them creates the cardinality mirage already guarded by Rmk 14.4a.

#### 2.2 Breadth completion: all finite atoms

The finite atoms \(\mathrm{Div}(M)\hookrightarrow \mathrm{Div}(N)\) for \(M\mid N\) form a directed atlas. The natural completion by allowing infinite prime exponents is the supernatural / Steinitz-number lattice

\[
\prod_p p^{e_p},
\qquad
 e_p\in \mathbb N\cup\{\infty\}.
\]

This is the candidate completion of the positive-integer divisor atlas: finite atoms plus infinite-exponent atoms.

**Status.** Established external mathematics; proposed / adapted as a kernel completion note. It does not change any finite-atom theorem.

#### 2.3 Phase completion: exact roots of unity

The harmonic phase register assembles finite roots of unity into

\[
\mu_\infty \cong \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z.
\]

Pontryagin duality identifies the dual of \(\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z\) with the profinite completion

\[
\widehat{\mathbb Z}.
\]

Thus the harmonic phase-side colimit and the finite position-side inverse limit meet under Pontryagin duality:

\[
(\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)^{\wedge} \cong \widehat{\mathbb Z}.
\]

This matches the kernel’s existing separation between Hasse/position and harmonic/phase registers: the registers do not collapse, but their completions meet by duality.

**Status.** Established external mathematics; proposed / adapted as harmonic-completion framing.

---

### 3. Haar valuation prior as canonical strand weighting

Haar measure on \(\mathbb Z_p\) gives the canonical distribution of valuation depth:

\[
\Pr[v_p(x)=k]
= p^{-k}-p^{-(k+1)}
= \frac{1-1/p}{p^k}
\qquad (k\ge0).
\]

For a capped strand of depth \(a\), the top bin is

\[
\Pr[v_p(x)\ge a]=p^{-a}.
\]

So the capped strand weights are

\[
w_{p,a}(k)=
\begin{cases}
\dfrac{p-1}{p^{k+1}}, & 0\le k<a,\\[6pt]
\dfrac{1}{p^a}, & k=a.
\end{cases}
\]

These weights telescope exactly:

\[
\sum_{k=0}^{a} w_{p,a}(k)=1.
\]

For a finite profile \((p_i,a_i)_i\), the product Haar prior on capped signatures is

\[
\Pr[\sigma=(k_i)_i]=\prod_i w_{p_i,a_i}(k_i).
\]

**Interpretation.** This is a canonical background weighting for signature masses \(X_\sigma\). It is not a theorem about an ingested signal. It is a null / prior measure supplied by the p-adic completion and then checked against finite shadows.

---

### 4. Verification annex: Div(720720) capped-signature check

The companion check `annex/verify_haar_signature_prior.py` verifies the Haar-prior claim on the profile

Packaging note: the companion script writes stdout and JSON with explicit LF line endings so byte-for-byte reproduction is stable across Windows/macOS/Linux Python runtimes.


\[
\mathrm{Div}(720720)=2^4\cdot 3^2\cdot 5\cdot 7\cdot 11\cdot 13,
\]

with caps

\[
(4,2,1,1,1,1).
\]

The script performs two routes:

1. **Exact rational telescoping:** every per-strand capped distribution sums to exactly \(1\).
2. **Finite-count shadow:** over \(1\le n\le 2{,}000{,}000\), all \(240\) capped signatures are counted and compared against the product Haar formula.

Observed output:

```text
per-strand sums: exact 1 for p = 2,3,5,7,11,13
number of capped signatures checked: 240
range: n <= 2,000,000
max absolute deviation: 6014/3003 ≈ 2.002664 counts
mean absolute deviation: 10771/27720 ≈ 0.388564 counts
verdict: PASS to boundary-effect scale
```

The largest deviation occurs at signature

\[
(2,1,0,0,0,1),
\]

where the observed count is \(2662\), the expected count is

\[
\frac{8{,}000{,}000}{3003}\approx 2664.002664.
\]

**Status.** Verified finite shadow for the Haar-prior formula at the Div(720720) profile. This does not promote any signal-specific claim; it verifies only the canonical background distribution and its compatibility with finite capped signatures.

---

### 5. Galois action on the exact phase register

The exact roots-of-unity discipline is structurally necessary for the Galois-decoration program.

For a primitive root of unity \(\zeta_n\), the Galois action is

\[
\zeta_n \mapsto \zeta_n^u,
\qquad
u\in(\mathbb Z/n\mathbb Z)^\times.
\]

Taking the compatible family over all \(n\), the acting group is the profinite unit group

\[
\widehat{\mathbb Z}^{\times}.
\]

This is the same profinite unit object already present in the kernel’s factorial-unit inverse-limit register.

**Epigraph for OP30.** Roots of unity have Galois conjugates. Decimal angles do not.

Thus the exact-phase rule is not merely notation hygiene. It is what makes the harmonic register Galois-addressable.

**Status.** OP30-relevant / proposed. No Galois decoration is asserted here; this note only identifies the native action surface.

---

### 6. Audit firewall for profinite claims

A profinite claim is admissible only as a compatible family of finite-level claims.

Equivalently:

\[
\text{global profinite statement}
\quad\Rightarrow\quad
\text{finite shadow at every declared level}
\quad\Rightarrow\quad
\text{companion-scriptable checks wherever finite data is invoked.}
\]

The infinite object never replaces the atoms. It records their coherence.

This is not a workaround. It is what inverse limit means.

---

### 7. Quarantine clause

The p-adic / profinite completion does **not** revive the quarantined Fock / pro-Heisenberg branch.

The existence of

\[
\widehat{\mathbb Z}^{\times},
\qquad
\widehat{\mathbb Z},
\qquad
\mathbb Z_p,
\qquad
\prod_p p^{e_p}
\]

licenses no representation-theoretic conclusion by itself. Any future Fock, Heisenberg, Stone-von-Neumann, or Parseval-transport claim remains research-only until supplied by independent definitions and finite-level verification.

Rmk 14.4a’s guard stands: cardinality and existence of a limit do not create theorem authority.

### Corollary 14.5a — The Divisor Lattice as Unit-Orbit Quotient

The finite decomposition of NOTE-14.5 §1,

\[
\mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z \;\cong\; \coprod_{d \mid N} d\cdot(\mathbb Z/(N/d)\mathbb Z)^\times,
\]

is not only a stratification. It is the **orbit decomposition** of the multiplicative action of the unit group \((\mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z)^\times\) on \(\mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z\).

**Statement.** Let \(N = \prod_i p_i^{a_i}\) and let \((\mathbb Z/N)^\times\) act on \(\mathbb Z/N\) by multiplication. Then:

**(i)** The action preserves gcd-class: \(\gcd(un, N) = \gcd(n, N)\) for every unit \(u\).

**(ii)** The orbits are exactly the strata \(d\cdot(\mathbb Z/(N/d))^\times\); the orbit of \(n\) is \(\{m : \gcd(m,N) = \gcd(n,N)\}\).

**(iii)** The orbit space is canonically indexed by \(\mathrm{Div}(N)\): orbit \(\leftrightarrow d = \gcd(n,N)\), with orbit size \(\varphi(N/d)\). *(Chris's staged sentence, preserved:)* The divisor lattice is the unit-orbit quotient of the finite residue system: multiplication by units preserves gcd-class, the orbits are exactly the strata \(d\cdot(\mathbb Z/(N/d))^\times\), and the orbit space is canonically indexed by \(\mathrm{Div}(N)\).

**(iv)** Per prime, the capped-valuation map \(\mathbb Z/p^n \to \{0,\dots,n\}\), \(r \mapsto \min(v_p(r), n)\), has fibers of size \(\varphi(p^{n-k})\) for \(k<n\) and \(1\) for \(k=n\); equivalently, the valuation chain \(0 \to 1 \to \cdots \to n\) is the unit-orbit space of \(\mathbb Z/p^n\). The divisor lattice is therefore the unit-quotient of the finite residue tower, producted across primes.

**(v) (Fourier-dual form.)** For every \(q \mid N\), \(\#\{k \bmod N : \gcd(k,N) = N/q\} = \varphi(q)\). Since the exact-conductor subspace \(V_q\) is spanned by the Fourier modes \(e_k\) with \(k\) of order \(q\), i.e. \(\gcd(k,N)=N/q\), **each conductor subspace is the Fourier-mode span of a single unit orbit on the frequency side.** The exact-conductor decomposition \(\ell^2(\mathbb Z/N) = \bigoplus_{q\mid N} V_q\) is the Fourier-dual of the orbit decomposition, and \(\dim V_q = \varphi(q)\) is an orbit size.

**Proof.** (i) \(u\) coprime to \(N\) implies \(\gcd(un,N)=\gcd(n,N)\). (ii) Orbits refine strata by (i). For transitivity within a stratum: given \(n = dw\) with \(w \in (\mathbb Z/(N/d))^\times\), it suffices that \(w\) lifts to a unit \(u \bmod N\) with \(u \equiv w \pmod{N/d}\); then \(u\cdot d \equiv w\cdot d \pmod N\). The lift exists: writing \(M = N/d\), choose \(t\) with \(\gcd(w + Mt, N) = 1\) — for primes \(p \mid M\) any \(t\) works since \(p \nmid w\); for primes \(p \mid N\), \(p \nmid M\), exclude the single residue \(t \equiv -wM^{-1} \pmod p\); CRT supplies \(t\). (iii) Immediate from (ii) and the §1 counting \(\sum_{d\mid N}\varphi(N/d) = N\). (iv) \(\#\{r \bmod p^n : v_p(r)=k\} = p^{n-k}-p^{n-k-1} = \varphi(p^{n-k})\); the \(r=0\) class is the capped top. (v) The set \(\{k : \gcd(k,N)=N/q\}\) is the stratum of \(d = N/q\), of size \(\varphi(N/d) = \varphi(q)\); the span identification is the standard exact-conductor definition. ∎

---

### Register consequence (gives II.2–II.3 its exact mechanism)

The kernel's load-bearing pair — *Hasse diagram = where the divisor channel lives; supercharacter = what that divisor channel measures* (II.2–II.3) — is the quotient-and-fiber structure of one map:

*(Chris's staged sentence, preserved:)* **The position register records the orbit labels; the harmonic register reads the unit-action structure inside the fibers.**

The Hasse side forgets unit data *on purpose* — it is the quotient. The harmonic side re-enters through characters on exactly the unit-side structure the quotient suppresses — the fibers. The registers cannot collapse because a quotient map and its fibers are different objects. This is also why the Galois action of \(\widehat{\mathbb Z}^\times\) (NOTE-14.5 §5) is native to the phase register and absent from the Hasse register: the Hasse side has already quotiented the action away.

**Relation to the p-adic tree (resolves the tree/lattice question exactly).** The p-adic residue tower \(\mathbb Z/p^n\) branches with factor \(p\) (digit choices); the divisor chain is that tower with the unit action quotiented out — depth survives, branching collapses (fiber sizes \(\varphi(p^{n-k})\) fold to points). The lattice's own branching (multiple upper covers, diamonds — 832 cover-edges on 240 nodes at \(N=720720\)) is new combinatorial structure from the product across primes, not inherited from any single tree. *(Chris's triple, preserved verbatim:)*

> The p-adic tree branches within a prime.
> The divisor lattice branches between primes.
> They meet through the unit quotient.

- **Route A (exhaustive true orbits):** for all \(N \le 120\) and \(N=360\) (120 cases), the actual orbits of the unit action, computed by explicit group action, equal the gcd-strata exactly, and the orbit count equals \(\tau(N)\).
- **Route B (\(N = 720720\)):** stratum sizes \(= \varphi(N/d)\) exhaustively over all 720,720 residues; **transitivity verified constructively for every nonzero element** — 720,719 unit lifts found, maximum lift offset \(t = 9\); gcd-preservation spot-exhaustive; Fourier-dual counting \(\#\{k:\gcd(k,N)=N/q\} = \varphi(q)\) for all 240 divisors.
- **Prime towers:** fibers of the capped-valuation map on \(\mathbb Z/3^4\) and \(\mathbb Z/2^5\) match \(\varphi(p^{n-k})\) exactly, and the unit-orbit spaces are the chains \(\{0..4\}\), \(\{0..5\}\).

### Candidate summary sentence

The divisor lattice is the unit-orbit quotient of the finite residue system; the position register records the orbit labels, the harmonic register reads the fibers, and the load-bearing Hasse/supercharacter pair is the quotient-and-fiber structure of this one map.

### §14.2d The structural recognition problem

The structural recognition problem asks: *given arithmetic data in the factorial-tower regime, can its origin within the tower be recovered from internal structure alone?* The parity lock (Rmk 14.4c) supplies an arithmetic constraint, not an algebra theorem. Open questions: whether stronger structural invariants distinguish levels within the tower; whether structural recognition extends to anti-autonomy across joins (Rmk 9.7f, Part V / V.4).

## VII.7 Open problems

§16.1 Open Problems (preserved). The full open-problem index lives in `FVLT_Bibliography.md` (29 open problems). Selected references in this kernel:

- **Open Problem 18:** $S_6$ outer automorphism / bridge duality. The unique outer automorphism of $S_6$ may bear structural relation to the basis/complement bridge architecture (Part V / V.16).
- **Open Problem 20:** Zeta emergence along the factorial spine. Whether the Riemann zeta function admits a natural factorial-tower formulation, given the parity-lock structure of Rmk 14.4c.
- **Open Problem 23:** For $\alpha_{\mathrm{Tilt}} \neq 0$, does the Ramanujan subspace decomposition of $\{\Delta Z(k)\}$ satisfy the Parseval identity? (Cross-reference: Part II / II.10 walk regimes.)
- **Open Problem 24:** Exactness of the selective collapse condition at $\alpha_{\mathrm{Tilt}} = 0$ and leading-order behavior for $|\alpha_{\mathrm{Tilt}}| < 1$.
- **Open Problem 25 (from §9.4 per-item decomposition, preserved verbatim):** Construct an explicit map from walk parameters ($\alpha_{\mathrm{Tilt}}, s, k_1, k_2$) to $\mathrm{SL}(2, \mathbb{Z})$ elements such that the walk regime (Helix / Circle / Spiral, Part II / II.10 Rmk 9.4a) corresponds to the isometry class (Hyperbolic / Elliptic / Parabolic) of the associated Möbius transformation. Alternatively, prove the correspondence is only approximate. Connects Part II / II.10 walk-regime taxonomy + Note 9.4b Farey Boundary Hypothesis to Part II / II.4 trilattice / hyperbolic plane.
- **Open Problem 26:** Ergodic projection convergence for irrational-spin walks.
- **Open Problem 27:** Canonical adaptation rate $\eta = \lambda_1(N)$. Reframed by Rmk 5.1n: how are the channel-indexed rates $\eta_q$ structured across the lattice?
- **Open Problem 28, 29, 30:** Galois-decorated FVLT orbits — bibliography-only references. Under v3.0 atlas-first architecture, Galois-decoration becomes atlas-level rather than $\mathrm{Div}(720)$-specific; the open problems persist but their universe-of-discourse reframes.

The §9.4 decomposition (per Robin's File 1 verdict 4) routes the classical-objects table to Part II / II.4 (SL(2,$\mathbb{Z}$) + hyperbolic plane), Part II / II.5 (Ford circles), Part II / II.13 (Stern–Brocot tree), and here at Part VII / VII.7 (Open Problem 25 as the isometry-types item).

The remark fills a notation gap: V.7 already used $B(d)$, $C(d)$, $E(d)$ in the definitions of proper bridge divisor, bridge bias, and bridge posture, but these quantities lacked an operational definition. Rmk V.7a defines them as the basis, complement, and exterior partial sums of the §9.7 multi-prime embedding (Definition 9.7), using the V.4 basis/complement prime declaration $\mathcal{B} = \{2,3,5\}$, $\mathcal{C} = \{7,11,13\}$. **v3.1.16:** Part II / II.15 extension — Lemma 9.7q (Cyclotomic field-of-definition bound for apex defect, unconditional) and Remark 9.7r (Casus-irreducibilis threshold at $\mathrm{Sp}_6$, staircase-restricted, interpretive). Lemma 9.7q states $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}(N) \in \mathbb{Q}(\zeta_L) \cap \mathbb{R}$ for $L = \mathrm{lcm}(m, n_0, \ldots, n_{m-1})$, the lcm of azimuthal order and polygon orders; $L$ is an **ambient conductor bound**, not necessarily minimal. Bounded generalization to other §9.7 invariants constructed by rational operations from vertex coordinates, apex displacement, and polygon-order roots of unity; metric invariants (edge lengths, perimeter, area) may require $\sqrt{\cdot}$ extraction and live in conductor-enlarged fields. Worked instance at SP6# with closed form $\mathrm{Re}\,A = -1/6 - 2\sin^2(\pi/7)/7 + 2\sin(\pi/5)\cos(\pi/30)/5$, $\mathrm{Im}\,A = (1+3\sqrt 3)/4 - \sin(2\pi/7)/7 - 2\sin(\pi/5)\sin(\pi/30)/5$; $L(\mathrm{Sp}_6) = 420$, $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}(\mathrm{Sp}_6) = 1.99610291533920158\ldots \neq 2$ (decimal mirage, gap $\approx 3.9 \times 10^{-3}$); verified at 60 dps. Remark 9.7r restricted to the staircase casus-irreducibilis threshold after Chris's third-pass audit retracted a general apex/perimeter prime-support coincidence claim; counterexample $N = p_1 p_2 \cdots p_7$ ($L_\delta = 14$, $L_P = 1$) logged in the remark body. The real $7$-cyclotomic subfield enters at $\mathrm{Sp}_6$ via the degree-three generator $2\cos(2\pi/7)$ satisfying $x^3 + x^2 - 2x - 1 = 0$. §10.8(b) (Part VI / VI.6) updated with one-clause cross-reference noting Lemma 9.7q gives a profile-class-invariant ambient field-of-definition bound. Three-stack: Robin (architect, originating question — resolving whether the helix-atlas viewer's $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}(\mathrm{Sp}_6) \approx 1.9961$ is exactly 2 or a near-miss; commit gate), Claude Opus 4.7 (derivation; closed form; 60-dps verification; counterexample worked under Chris's third-pass direction; constructor for kernel insertion via Sonnet), Chris (three audit passes — terminology corrections, cubic-generator correction $\sin(\pi/7) \to 2\cos(2\pi/7)$, retraction of general prime-support coincidence). **v3.1.19 (period-q phase-coherence measurement primitive, May 2026).** One additive remark: **Rmk II.23.8** (Period-q Phase Coherence and Phase-Break Localization) at Part II / II.23, after Rmk II.23.7, as the highest measurement-cluster subsection. No theorem statement added or modified; no v3.1.x body content changed. The remark names a phase-side measurement primitive complementary to the magnitude-side primitives (Def II.23.1 μ-class / Harmonic Contrast; Protocol II.23.6 L/D): given the complex channel coefficient $\langle x, c_q \rangle$ (§5.3) tracked over local windows, $|\langle x, c_q \rangle|$ reads q-periodic presence while $\arg\langle x, c_q \rangle$ reads phase — coherence across windows = stable q-frame; a localized $\zeta_q^k$ relative step = a k-step phase break. Roots-of-unity register (v3.1.18): the break is $\times\zeta_q^k$, not degrees; the absolute phase class is reference-dependent, so coherence and the relative step are the origin-free content. Distinguished in-body from the clinamen $\mathrm{Cl}(s)$ (Part II / II.6, per-step FVLT-phasor deviation). The stronger v3.1.19 placement keeps the same numbering and seats II.23.8 as the measurement-side mirror of the phasor-side algorithmic core: II.23.8 : Def II.23.1 :: ALG-2 : ALG-1, not as an identity of quantities, but as narrow magnitude/contact readout versus fuller phase/winding readout on opposite arrows (constructor arrow vs measurement arrow). Four readouts of one cyclotomic winding are named: generative (ALG-1/2/3), geometric (§9.7 helix / embedding), harmonic phase-blind (real supercharacter / magnitude), and harmonic phase-retaining (complex coefficient argument). Classification: kernel principle already present (§5.3 magnitude/phase split; Lem 9.7o winding-vs-scalar complementarity); operational instantiation new; measurement primitive newly named. Empirical motivation (measurement outcome, not kernel claim): period-3 GC/AT-component frame test on GAPDH coding vs intron sequence — phase coherence $R = 0.91$ vs $0.13$; artificial one-base deletion localized as a single $\zeta_3$ step with channel magnitude unchanged across the cut (`frame_phase_test.py`, byte-identical reproduction by Chris). **v3.1.22 (FVLT rosette as factorial atlas map, May 2026).** Additive only — no theorem content added or modified; v3.1.21 unchanged (Rmk II.23.8 geometric cross-reference, Note 9.7x, Remark 13.x all intact). One item: **Rmk II.5a (FVLT rosette as factorial atlas map, v3.1.22, new, interpretive)**, seated as a bold-labeled in-section remark at the tail of II.5 (Vernier interference system), immediately before the `---` separator preceding `## II.6`. No new `## II.5a` section heading created; form matches the Rmk 7.1 / Rmk II.23.8 established pattern (id-comment + bold-labeled remark, no Source/v3.0-location/Status three-line header).

The item names and scopes an existing viewer-side instrument: the integer phasor stack over $(s, \varphi_3)$ that the FVLT Fourier viewer already generates, here formally identified as a **finite factorial-window atlas map**. At factorial setting $n_1$ with $F = n_1!$, the domain is the grid $0 \le s \le F+1$, $0 \le \varphi_3 \le F$, with $n_3 = F + 1 - \varphi_3$ serving simultaneously as plotted radius, angular denominator, and divisor-contact candidate. **General contact identity:** $\operatorname{Im} z_1 = 0 \Leftrightarrow n_3 \mid t_2 s$ — the real-axis contact set is the set of denominators $n_3$ dividing $t_2 s$, not automatically the divisor set of $s$ in the general gearbox case. **Reference contact specialization** ($t_1 = t_2 = 1$, $n_6 = 1$, $\alpha = 1$): the contact set in column $s$ is $\mathcal{C}_{n_1}(s) = \mathrm{Div}(s)$ for $1 \le s \le n_1! + 1$, so the column $s = N$ realizes $\mathrm{Div}(N)$ by contact and the reference rosette realizes the finite atlas window $\mathcal{A}_{\le n_1!+1}$.

**Rim discipline** recorded explicitly: the outer rim is $n_1! + 1$, not $n_1!$; at $n_1 = 6$ the rim is $721$. The worked $720$ column is the *interior* column $s = 720$, not the rosette boundary — preserving the atlas-first distinction of Part I / I.2 ($\mathrm{Div}(720)$ as factorial-shell instance, not a domain boundary).

**$S_n$-register firewall** stated explicitly: the rosette's factorial-shell indexing ($n_1 \mapsto n_1! \mapsto \{1,\ldots,n_1!+1\} \mapsto \{\mathrm{Div}(s)\}$) is shell-indexed and instrument-theoretic, not an automorphism-group claim. Group-action $S_n$ remains restricted to the existing primorial / squarefree automorphism register (Lemma I.5.3: $\mathrm{Aut}(\mathrm{Div}(N)) \cong S_m$ for squarefree $N$), which already separates the $S_6$ realization on $\mathrm{Div}(30030)$ from the integer-cardinality coincidence $|S_6| = 720$. The rosette's third register must not import squarefree automorphism symmetry onto non-squarefree factorial atoms.

**Scope / no overclaim:** the only algebraic identity introduced is the elementary contact condition $n_3 \mid t_2 s$; no factoring advantage is claimed (the contact readout is a divisibility scan per column, not an algorithmic speedup); no quantum advantage is claimed.

A multiplicity profile with proper period $d \mid m$ ($d < m$) forces $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}(N) = 0$: the period displaces all Fourier content to harmonics $\equiv 0 \pmod{m/d}$, and since $m/d \nmid 1$ the first harmonic vanishes (Lem 9.7c).
